Without a counterpart in these examples: a model where an SExp is expected (an argument of dist or of lift, the result, a model inside a Scheme value) and a value where a model is expected (a number, a thunk, a branch of if_), for mcmC and exact-posterior alike (check-model), which Haskell's types reject since a Model is a type of its own (plan §15 C32); a model or an SExp that a lifted function answers, whatever the node it feeds, at the build or at an update, or a Scheme value it answers that holds one, walked when the function first answers, at the build (check-lifted-value); an observation that is a model or a Scheme value holding an SExp (CondV a = a, S:180-193), or that a distribution constructor computes from its argument, raised by the update that changes it, where upstream keeps the observation the node was built with (S:251-252, M:487-488) and so silently observes the first value; a Scheme container of SExps as the result, which the engine would otherwise take for a constant (deviation 9), at any depth of pairs and vectors, and likewise inside the value of a diracN or of an argument of dist, also next to an SExp argument, where the pair or the list of the arguments hides it from the node's construction (the Show of the sample type, S:144-151); a distribution constructor that answers something other than a distribution (plan §7), a distribution given arguments, and a value given to dist that is neither a distribution nor a constructor; a sample count that is not an exact integer (mcmC :: Integer -> Model a -> [a], S:196) or is negative, for each of mcmC, mcmC-fold and mcmC§ (deviation 10 for a negative one: upstream retries the build forever or answers an infinite list, M:885-891, M:897-898); a #:check that is neither a boolean nor a step count; and a node of an earlier run, which a model captured with set!, used alone (check-owner) or lifted together with a node of the current run, whose NT would mix two chains. A node captured in a branch escapes it as a parent of a node outside (branch-escape), and also as the result of a sibling if_'s branch, which that if_'s exit would read, or as the model's result: each raises branch (deviation 10), for mcmC and exact-posterior alike, whose walks rely on it to skip the inactive nodes. The errors of an if_'s test, a node of an earlier run or a Val that holds a model or an SExp, name if_. An observation that a constructor recomputes as a fresh value equal to the first, a list or a flonum, is unchanged: both chains sample with #:check, and enumeration gives P(x) = .6. dist takes any number of arguments (deviation 9): three, combined with collect, give the chain of the equivalent two-argument constructor; but as many as its constructor takes, which upstream's Args family makes part of the type (S:137-151): (dist normal 0.), a nullary dist of a one-argument constructor and three arguments to a two-argument one raise construction, located at dist, also in an inactive branch in 'extended mode, which would otherwise take CHICKEN's arity error for a rejection of the build (C22) and silently empty the chain, and under enumeration; an arity error raised inside the constructor's own body is a Scheme error. A model built and discarded, such as (dist bern .5) evaluated outside any bind, makes no node, as in Haskell, and raises nothing.

The case sampleable-entry runs the engine's ifnode directly, on the chain eval-state threads: "Entry node for if should not be sampleable" (M:643-647). The set!s run after the bind of the node they capture, so once per run (plan §15 C35, embedding notes). Not a kind: a bare clause that is not a model, (add1! k) between two binds, raises CHICKEN's own error when the model runs, a Scheme exn.

(define (test/language/errors _)
  (define captured #f)
  (define leaked/sibling
    (do/monad
      (← c (dist bern 0.5))
      (let leak #f)
      (if_ c
           (do/monad (← h (dist bern 0.9)) (let _ (set! leak h)) (return h))
           (return #f))
      (← y (if_ #t (return leak) (return #f)))
      (return (pair c y))))
  (define leaked/result
    (do/monad
      (← c (dist bern 0.5))
      (let leak #f)
      (if_ c
           (do/monad
             (← h (dist normal 10.0 1.0))
             (let _ (set! leak h))
             (return h))
           (return 0.0))
      (return leak)))
  (define cases

test/language/stream: pass

L8: mcmC§ is Haskell's lazy list of samples (M:897-906), an (aux stream) stream made by τ->§ from the engine's generator: its first 100 elements are (mcmC 100 pcond4). A model with no trace of finite log-likelihood gives the empty stream after #:retries builds (1000 by default; upstream's initial_step returns [], M:868, M:882-891). The budget is exact, as upstream's "repeat up to limit times" (M:882-891): at seed 17 the model rare, which observes #t from bern of 1 or 0 as its c is true or false, draws c false in its first six builds and true in the seventh, so six builds (mcmC's limit, or #:retries) find no trace and seven do.

(define (test/language/stream _)
  (let1 (s (mcmC§ pcond4))
        (⊦ equal? (mcmC 100 pcond4) (§->list (take§ 100 s)))
        (⊦ equal? (cadr (mcmC 2 pcond4)) (car§ (cdr§ s))))
  (⊦ equal? '() (mcmC§ (dist (condition 5.0 uniform) 0.0 1.0)))
  (let1 (rare (do/monad
                (← c (dist bern 0.1))
                (dist (condition #t bern) (lift (λ (cv) (if cv 1.0 0.0)) c))
                (return c)))
        (⊦ equal? '() (mcmC 6 rare))
        (⊦ equal? (make-list 7 #t) (mcmC 7 rare))
        (⊦ equal? '() (mcmC§ rare #:retries 6))
        (⊦ equal?
           (make-list 3 #t)
           (§->list (take§ 3 (mcmC§ rare #:retries 7))))))

test/language/three-interpretations: pass

One model, three interpretations (plan §15 C37): grass under exact enumeration (new: a Hansei search tree over the trace graph of one build, explored and normalized by probcc-explore and probcc-normalize), under likelihood weighting (new: forward sampling over the same graph) and under the incremental MH of the engine. The exact posterior is upstream's "theoretical" 0.4684714427203697 (E:322); likelihood weighting agrees within 0.02, the Markov chain, autocorrelated, within 0.04. The oracles suite of hakaru10-exact.scm runs this check on eleven models.

test/language/search-tree-paths: pass

A fourth reading of a model, written in this file over the search tree of model->search-tree, in the format of Hansei's probcc-reify/0 (aux.hansei.scm): the number of paths through a model whose choices have finite supports. The tree has a subtree (C thunk) per support point of each active choice of the trace graph and per active observation of positive weight and finite LL (an LL of NaN or +inf prunes its path, as one of weight 0 does), and a value (V v) at each leaf; forcing every subtree and counting the leaves gives grass the four paths of rain × sprinkler, pcond1 the four of x × the branch's choice, pcond4 one for x = 1 and two for x = 2 (the observation has one subtree), ph2 four.

(define (search-tree-paths m)
  (let paths ((tree (model->search-tree m)))
    (fold (λ (entry n)
             (+ n
                (match/first
                  (car entry)
                  ((V ,_) 1)
                  ((C ,thunk) (paths (thunk))))))
          0
          tree)))

(define (test/language/search-tree-paths _)
  (⊦ equal? '(4 4 3 4) (map search-tree-paths (list grass pcond1 pcond4 ph2))))

((eta 0.0) (memory #(12582912 6222888 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))

test/language/procedure-values: pass

L10: procedures as values. A random choice may draw procedures, and (diracN car) makes a Dirac node of one. A procedure is a plain Val (S:107 Val f), which a model passes on to lift: it is never taken for a model, a State computation of a type of its own (plan §15 C32). Exact enumeration, the chain (with and without #:check) and likelihood weighting agree; the counts at seed 17 are regression values.

(define procedure-choice
  (do/monad
    (← f (dist categorical (list (cons car 0.5) (cons cadr 0.5))))
    (return (lift (λ (g) (g '(1 2))) f))))

(define procedure-constant
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← f (diracN car))
    (return (lift (λ (g cv) (if cv (g '(1 2)) 0)) f c))))

A lifted function may also answer a closure it builds, which procedure-built keeps in a Dirac node: #:check recomputes it as a fresh closure and takes two procedures for the same value (their consumers, which apply them, are checked themselves), as it does for a model that answers such closures. A value may be circular: in circular-value the Dirac node holds a circular list, which the build's check for SExps inside a value (deviation 9) and the comparisons of #:check walk to an end. Both chains sample with #:check what they sample without it, in both semantics, and agree with exact enumeration.

(define procedure-built
  (do/monad
    (← k (dist uniformly '(1 2 3)))
    (← scale (diracN (lift (λ (kv) (λ (x) (* kv x))) k)))
    (return (lift (λ (f) (f 10)) scale))))

(define circular-value
  (do/monad
    (← b (dist bern 0.5))
    (← cyc (diracN (lift (λ (bv) (circular-list bv)) b)))
    (return (lift car cyc))))

The closure may also sit inside a record: in distribution-value a Dirac node holds a distribution (a distk, whose rdistr holds its sampler's and its density's closures), from which (dist identity d) draws x, and in record-value a record of the model's own and a promise. #:check walks a record's fields as a vector's and takes two promises for the same value (a forced one holds its value, a fresh one a closure), so their recomputations match, and the chains sample with it what they sample without it.

(define distribution-value
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← d
       (diracN
         (lift (λ (cv) (if cv (uniformly '(1 2)) (uniformly '(1 2 3 4)))) c)))
    (← x (dist identity d))
    (dist (condition #t bern) (lift (λ (xv) (if (odd? xv) 0.8 0.3)) x))
    (return (pair c x))))

(define record-value
  (do/monad
    (← k (dist uniformly '(1 2 3)))
    (← s (diracN (lift (λ (kv) (make-scaler (λ (x) (* kv x)))) k)))
    (← p (diracN (lift (λ (kv) (delay (* kv 10))) k)))
    (return (lift (λ (sv pv) (list ((scaler-f sv) 10) (force pv))) s p))))

(define (test/language/procedure-values _)
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     '((1 . 0.5) (2 . 0.5))
     (exact-posterior procedure-choice))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     '((0 . 0.5) (1 . 0.5))
     (exact-posterior procedure-constant))
  (⊦ equal? '((1 . 974) (2 . 1026)) (hist (mcmC 2000 procedure-choice)))
  (⊦ equal? '((0 . 1026) (1 . 974)) (hist (mcmC 2000 procedure-constant)))
  (⊦ equal?
     '(50 50)
     (list (length (mcmC 50 procedure-choice #:check #t))
           (length (mcmC 50 procedure-constant #:check #t))))
  (⊦ equal?
     '((1 2) (0 1))
     (list (map car (likelihood-weighting 100 procedure-choice))
           (map car (likelihood-weighting 100 procedure-constant))))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     (map (λ (v) (cons v (/ 1.0 3.0))) '(10 20 30))
     (exact-posterior procedure-built))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     '((#f . 0.5) (#t . 0.5))
     (exact-posterior circular-value))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     (map (Λ ((,v unquote w)) (cons v (/ w 11.0)))
          '(((#f . 1) . 2.0)
            ((#f . 2) . 0.75)
            ((#f . 3) . 2.0)
            ((#f . 4) . 0.75)
            ((#t . 1) . 4.0)
            ((#t . 2) . 1.5)))
     (exact-posterior distribution-value))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     (map (λ (v) (cons (list v v) (/ 1.0 3.0))) '(10 20 30))
     (exact-posterior record-value))
  (let1 (scales
          (do/monad
            (← k (dist uniformly '(1 2 3)))
            (diracN (lift (λ (kv) (λ (x) (* kv x))) k))))
        (⊦ equal?
           (map (λ (f) (f 10)) (mcmC 50 scales))
           (map (λ (f) (f 10)) (mcmC 50 scales #:check #t))))
  (for-each
    (λ (m)
       (⊦ equal? (mcmC 50 m) (mcmC 50 m #:check #t))
       (⊦ equal?
          (mcmC 50 m #:semantics 'extended)
          (mcmC 50 m #:semantics 'extended #:check #t)))
    (list procedure-built circular-value distribution-value record-value)))

(aux hakaru10): upstream records, bit for bit

Replays

Chains replayed bit-exactly against upstream's recorded runs (the comments of Examples1.hs, Syntax.hs and ColoredBalls.hs) and against the Python transcription of the engine (prototype regression values), with the random-1.0.1.1 StdGen of mkStdGen 17 (M:858). A replay holds only if the port draws exactly where upstream draws, in the same order, computes every Double expression in Haskell's association, and creates the nodes in the same order (plan §6.1, C18). The triage order for a failing replay: the generator, the samplers' draw order, the order of the resampleable nodes (M:239, M:962), floating-point order, node creation order, (de)activation sums.

test/replay/grass: pass

X3: 9363 rainy samples of 20000, 0.46815, upstream's "0.46815 for 20000 -- with old resampling" (E:321).

(define (nnot p b) (if b p 1.0))

(define ((nor strength-x strength-y noise) x y)
  (bern (- 1.0
           (* (* (nnot (- 1.0 strength-x) x) (nnot (- 1.0 strength-y) y))
              (- 1.0 noise)))))

(define grass
  (do/monad
    (← rain (dist bern 0.3))
    (← sprinkler (dist bern 0.5))
    (← grass-is-wet (dist (condition #t (nor 0.9 0.8 0.1)) rain sprinkler))
    (return rain)))

grass = do
  rain        <- dist bern 0.3
  sprinkler   <- dist bern 0.5
  --  dist (nor 0.9 0.8 0.1) rain sprinkler
  grass_is_wet <- dist (True `condition` nor 0.9 0.8 0.1) rain sprinkler
  return rain

-- noisy-or function
nor :: Double -> Double -> Double -> Bool -> Bool -> Distribution Bool
nor strengthX strengthY noise x y =
  bern $ 1 - nnot (1-strengthX) x * nnot (1-strengthY) y * (1-noise)
  
-- noisy not function
nnot :: Num a => a -> Bool -> a
nnot p True  = p
nnot p False = 1

grass_run = let ns = 20000
                r  = mcmC ns grass
            in
             ((fromIntegral . length . filter id $ r) /
              (fromIntegral ns :: Double))
-- 0.46815 for 20000 -- with old resampling
-- 0.4684714427203697  -- theoretical
(define (test/replay/grass _) (⊦ = 9363 (count-true (mcmC 20000 grass))))

((eta 0.069) (memory #(12582912 7162792 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))

test/replay/pcond1: pass

X4: both of upstream's records (E:112-114), of 1000 and 10000 samples (the first sums to 1000, though the code reads mcmC 10000).

(define (is1 x) (lift (λ (xv) (= xv 1)) x))

(define pcond1
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist categorical '((1 . 0.5) (2 . 0.5))))
    (if_ (is1 x)
         (dist categorical '((10 . 0.5) (11 . 0.5)))
         (dist categorical '((20 . 0.5) (21 . 0.5))))))

pcond1 = do
  x <- dist categorical (pure [(1,0.5), (2,0.5)])
  if_ ((==1) <$> x)
     (dist categorical (pure [(10,0.5), (11,0.5)]))
     (dist categorical (pure [(20,0.5), (21,0.5)]))

pcond1r = hist $ mcmC 10000 pcond1
-- fromList [(10,246),(11,222),(20,240),(21,292)]
-- fromList [(10,2481),(11,2394),(20,2565),(21,2560)]
(define (test/replay/pcond1 _)
  (⊦ equal?
     '((10 . 246) (11 . 222) (20 . 240) (21 . 292))
     (hist (mcmC 1000 pcond1)))
  (⊦ equal?

test/replay/pcorr: pass

X8: Yufei Cai's example (E:223-238; Figaro issue 548), a custom distribution conditioned on two random choices.

(define (pcorr* pfuzz result)
  (define ((fuzzy2 pfuzz op) x y)
    (let1 (v (op x y))
          (categorical
            `((,v unquote pfuzz) (,(not v) unquote (- 1.0 pfuzz))))))
  (define (xor y x) (not (eq? x y)))
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist bern 0.5))
    (← y (dist bern 0.5))
    (dist (condition #t (fuzzy2 pfuzz xor)) x y)
    (return (result x y))))

pcorr pfuzz = do
  x <- dist bern 0.5
  y <- dist bern 0.5
  dist (True `condition` fuzzy2 pfuzz xor) x y
  return y
 where
   -- Creating a custom distribution
   fuzzy2 pfuzz op x y = let v = op x y in
     categorical [(v,pfuzz),(not v,1-pfuzz)]
   xor y x = not (x == y)

_ = hist $ mcmC 1000 (pcorr 0.1)
-- fromList [(False,419),(True,581)]
(define (test/replay/pcorr _)
  (⊦ equal? '((#f . 419) (#t . 581)) (hist (mcmC 1000 (pcorr 0.1)))))

((eta 0.004) (memory #(12582912 6214952 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))

(define girl
  (let ()
    (define ((fuzzy2 pfuzz op) x y)
      (let1 (v (op x y))
            (categorical
              `((,v unquote (- 1.0 pfuzz)) (,(not v) unquote pfuzz)))))
    (do/monad
      (← girl1 (dist bern 0.5))
      (← girl2 (dist bern 0.5))
      (dist (condition #t (fuzzy2 0.01 (λ (a b) (or a b)))) girl1 girl2)
      (return girl1))))

girl = do
  girl1 <- dist bern 0.5
  girl2 <- dist bern 0.5
  dist (True `condition` fuzzy2 0.01 (||)) girl1 girl2
  return girl1
 where
   -- Creating a custom distribution
   fuzzy2 pfuzz op x y = let v = op x y in
     categorical [(v,1-pfuzz),(not v,pfuzz)]

_ = length . filter id $ mcmC 100 girl

(define (post-bias c)
  (do/monad
    (← coin (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ coin
         (dist (condition c normal) 0.0 1.0)
         (dist (condition c normal) 100.0 1.0))
    (return coin)))

post_bias c = do
  coin <- dist bern 0.5
  if_ coin (dist (c `condition` normal) 0 1)
           (dist (c `condition` normal) 100 1)
  return coin

_ = mcmC 10 (post_bias 1)
-- All true

(define (biased-coin c1 c2)
  (do/monad
    (← b (dist beta 1.0 1.0))
    (dist (condition c1 bern) b)
    (dist (condition c2 bern) b)
    (return b)))

biased_coin c1 c2 = do
  b <- dist beta 1 1

(aux hakaru10): the laws of model composition

Laws

The equational laws of model composition (P §2.1 l.250-281, §4.3 l.803-843): Dirac is the left and the right unit of composition, and an unused variable is marginalized. Under the MCMC engine the right unit law holds sample for sample (X2, X16); the left unit law and marginalization hold in distribution. Under exact enumeration, which walks the built graph and reuses no incremental machinery (plan §6.10), they hold exactly.

do {x ← p; diracN x} ≡ p,  do {x ← diracN v; p} ≡ let x = v in p

test/laws/left-unit: pass

A1: do {x <- diracN 5; dist normal x 1} is dist normal 5 1 sample for sample: the constant Dirac node draws nothing and is never proposed (P l.259-261).

(define (test/laws/left-unit _)
  (⊦ equal?
     (mcmC 200 (dist normal 5.0 1.0))
     (mcmC 200 (do/monad (← x (diracN 5.0)) (dist normal x 1.0)))))

((eta 0.001) (memory #(25165824 6212720 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))

test/laws/exact: pass

The laws under exact enumeration, where a model is its distribution: the right and left units, marginalization, and a Dirac node introduced for a lifted parameter (ph2, whose posterior is 0.58, E:91). Enumeration walks the built graph; it reuses no incremental machinery (plan §6.10): a Dirac node's value is recomputed from its parents' on each path.

(define (test/laws/exact _)
  (let ((coin (dist bern 0.4))
        (pmarg/coin
          (do/monad (← x (dist bern 0.4)) (← y (dist bern 0.3)) (return y))))
    (⊦ equal?
       (exact-posterior coin)
       (exact-posterior (do/monad (← x (dist bern 0.4)) (diracN x))))
    (⊦ equal?
       (exact-posterior (dist bern 0.3))
       (exact-posterior (do/monad (← p (diracN 0.3)) (dist bern p))))
    (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-15)
       (exact-posterior (dist bern 0.3))
       (exact-posterior pmarg/coin))
    (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-15)
       (exact-posterior ph2)
       (exact-posterior
         (do/monad
           (← x (dist bern 0.2))
           (← q (diracN (lift (λ (xv) (if xv 0.1 0.7)) x)))
           (dist bern q))))))

(aux hakaru10): the semantics of branch reactivation

Semantics of branches

In upstream's kernel an inactive node's updates are delayed (M:298-303) and replayed when its branch is activated again (M:767-775). For an observed node this is exact: activation adds back the LL that deactivation took. For a resampleable node the replayed update adds ll(now) − ll(stale) to the Δ of the switching step: it charges the change of LL of steps already accepted without it. The note M:1145-1168 justifies the treatment of branches by merging a branch's nodes into one node:

So, when it comes to computing the acceptance ratio for a program
with branches, we take

p4 = do
     c <- bernN (Val 0.5)
     if_ c (do e1 e2 ...)
           (do e3 e4 e5 ...)

to be

p4' = do
      c <- bernN (Val 0.5)
      if_ c e_then e_else

That is, we merge (at least for the purpose of presentation) e1, e2 ...
into one node e_then, whose ll is the sum of ll of constituents. Ditto
for e_else.

When we switch the branches, we remove the ll of one branch from
llcum and add ll of the other branch. In our terms, we remove the ll
of the deactivated nodes and add ll of the activated nodes.

This algorithm is resilient with respect to adding replacing a node e
with e >>= diracN -- the desired behavior.

The argument assumes the reactivated nodes are fresh draws from their prior, while the kernel reuses their stale values. The smallest counterexample, pswitch (plan §6.8), has P(c) = 0.5; the exact stationary distribution of upstream's kernel gives P(c) = 0.377286. The upstream examples are unaffected: their branch-local resampleable nodes have constant parameters.

(define pswitch
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← m (dist uniformly '(0 1)))
    (if_ c
         (dist categorical (lift (λ (mv) `((,mv . 0.9) (,(- 1 mv) . 0.1))) m))
         (diracN (lift identity m)))
    (return c)))

The opt-in #:semantics 'extended (deviation 8) runs MH on the space extended with the inactive resampleable nodes as auxiliary variables drawn from their prior, the merged-node argument made exact: updates are never delayed, an inactive resampleable node's LL change counts in Δ and an inactive observed node's does not, (de)activation still moves only observed weights, and the proposal, the retry and the cardinality term are upstream's. An inactive node whose update raises a parameter error, or any other error that an inactive Dirac node would defer, rejects the step (plan C22). The kernel residual of hakaru10-exact.scm shows that the extended kernel leaves the posterior invariant, exactly, on small models.

test/semantics/check: pass

B4: #:check #t on 300 samples of seven models, in both semantics, raises no condition: a rebuild of the model reproduces the initial trace node by node (graph, values, and LLs within 1e-9), an address-order redraw of its random choices reproduces their values and the trace's LL (plan §6.9, §15 C37; within 1e-9 of the sum of the LLs' magnitudes, so that far-inactive, whose build at seed 17 deactivates an observation of LL about -5·10⁷, passes in both semantics: the 'upstream trace's LL adds and then subtracts it, M:659, and the 'extended one never counts it), and after every step the reference evaluator recomputes every node from scratch (activity counts, Dirac values, distributions and LLs, exits) and finds the incremental state, a rejected step restores the state exactly, and in 'extended mode an accepted step's Δ is the change of the extended target's LL; the next case shows that each of these checks fails when it should.

The rebuild builds the branch its test does not select as the first build does, under lazy lifting, so a model whose inactive branch applies car to the '() its test rules out (a deferred Dirac value, M:99, M:120-129) passes the check, with a constant test and with a lifted one, in both semantics, and samples what it samples without #:check:

(define guarded-car/constant
  (do/monad
    (let xs '())
    (← b (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ (lift null? xs) (return b) (diracN (lift car xs)))))

(define guarded-car/node
  (do/monad
    (← b (dist bern 0.5))
    (let xs (lift (λ (bv) (if bv '() '(1))) b))
    (if_ (lift null? xs) (return 'none) (diracN (lift car xs)))))

Three models reach the pending weight of the 'extended semantics (deviation 8), each checked on 2000 samples. In act-update and act-update/finite a step that moves x from 2 activates the observation and updates its parameters at once: its weight is added once, with its new LL, when propagation ends, which the step check verifies against the change of the extended LL (the stale LL is -inf in the first, log .5 in the second). In pending-abort the step that activates the observation is then rejected mid-propagation by the inactive uniformly '() of the second branch (plan C22): the journal restores the pending flag, which the reference evaluator finds clear and the step check finds restored. Its chain, which the extended target keeps off x = 0 (as in invalid-inactive, case B5), gives P(x = 1) = .3/1.3 within .02 over 20000 samples. The 'extended chain of act-update gives its exact posterior, (.9 .3 1)/2.2, within .02 over 20000 samples, and the kernel residual (case E10 of hakaru10-exact.scm) is below 10⁻¹² on both act-update models. Counting the weight at activation, or again at the update, makes the residual of act-update about .18, and its chain never leaves x = 2.

(define act-update
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist uniformly '(0 1 2)))
    (if_ (lift (λ (v) (< v 2)) x)
         (dist (condition 0 categorical)
               (lift (λ (v)
                        (case v
                          ((0) '((0 . 0.9) (1 . 0.1)))
                          ((1) '((0 . 0.3) (1 . 0.7)))
                          (else '((1 . 1.0)))))
                     x))
         (return 'none))
    (return x)))

(define act-update/finite
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist uniformly '(0 1 2)))
    (if_ (lift (λ (v) (< v 2)) x)
         (dist (condition 0 categorical)
               (lift (λ (v)
                        (case v
                          ((0) '((0 . 0.9) (1 . 0.1)))
                          ((1) '((0 . 0.3) (1 . 0.7)))
                          (else '((0 . 0.5) (1 . 0.5)))))
                     x))
         (return 'none))
    (return x)))

(define pending-abort
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist uniformly '(0 1 2)))
    (if_ (lift (λ (v) (< v 2)) x)
         (dist (condition 0 categorical)
               (lift (λ (v)
                        `((0 unquote (if (= v 0) 0.9 0.3))
                          (1 unquote (if (= v 0) 0.1 0.7))))
                     x))
         (return 'none))
    (if_ (lift (λ (v) (not (= v 0))) x)
         (dist uniformly (lift (λ (v) (if (= v 0) '() '(a b))) x))
         (return 'z))
    (return x)))

The chain keeps an initial trace of LL NaN, which only an infinite LL would make it rebuild (Metropolis.hs:853-854, 882-891): at seed 17 nan-initial's build draws b = 3, whose observation has LL NaN. A step that moves b has a NaN ratio, which fails both acceptance tests (Metropolis.hs:939-944), so b stays 3, while c, whose steps leave the NaN term alone, takes both values. The step check of #:check cannot compare a Δ with a change of NaN extended LLs and skips it there, so both chains sample with it what they sample without it.

(define nan-initial
  (do/monad
    (← b (dist uniformly '(1 2 3)))
    (dist (condition 0.0 normal)
          0.0
          (lift (λ (bv) (case bv ((3) 0.0) ((2) 1.0) (else 2.0))) b))
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (return (pair b c))))

A value of NaN is equal? to no flonum, not even to itself, and a recomputation builds a fresh one, so the checks match two NaNs, in a value and in a distribution's parameters (same-value?): the 300 samples of nan-mean are the same with and without #:check, in both semantics, and the rebuild check passes at seed 107336, where k = 0 and the mean is NaN.

(define nan-mean
  (do/monad
    (let mean-of (λ (kv) (/ (exact->inexact (* kv 3)) (exact->inexact kv))))
    (← k (dist uniformly '(0 1 2)))
    (← mean (diracN (lift mean-of k)))
    (← z
       (dist categorical
             (lift (λ (kv) `((,(mean-of kv) . 0) (a . 1) (b . 1))) k)))
    (return (pair k z))))

(define (test/semantics/check _)
  (for-each
    (λ (semantics)
       (for-each
         (Λ ((,name ,m))
            (⊦ equal?
               (list name semantics 300)
               (list name
                     semantics
                     (length (mcmC 300 m #:semantics semantics #:check #t)))))
         `((phier3 ,(phierN 3))
           (mhex ,mhex)

           (colored-balls ,(cballs-model '(blue green blue)))
           (pswitch ,pswitch)
           (regression ,(regression (regression-data 2 10))))))
    '(upstream extended))
  (for-each
    (λ (semantics)
       (for-each
         (Λ ((,name ,m))
            (⊦ equal?
               (list name semantics (mcmC 50 m #:semantics semantics))
               (list name
                     semantics
                     (mcmC 50 m #:semantics semantics #:check #t))))
         `((guarded-car/constant ,guarded-car/constant)
           (guarded-car/node ,guarded-car/node))))
    '(upstream extended))
  (for-each
    (Λ ((,name ,m))
       (⊦ equal?
          (list name 2000)

    `((act-update ,act-update)
      (act-update/finite ,act-update/finite)
      (pending-abort ,pending-abort)))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 0.02)
     `((1 unquote (/ 0.3 1.3)) (2 unquote (/ 1.0 1.3)))
     (proportions (mcmC 20000 pending-abort #:semantics 'extended)))
  (let1 (far-inactive
          (do/monad
            (← c (dist bern 0.5))
            (if_ (lift not c)
                 (do/monad (dist (condition 1000.0 normal) 0.0 0.1) (return 1))
                 (return 0))))
        (for-each

  (let1 (exact (exact-posterior act-update))
        (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
           `((0 unquote (/ 0.9 2.2))
             (1 unquote (/ 0.3 2.2))
             (2 unquote (/ 1.0 2.2)))
           exact)
        (⊦ (equal-approx? 0.02)
           exact
           (proportions (mcmC 20000 act-update #:semantics 'extended))))
  (for-each
    (λ (semantics)
       (let1 (xs (mcmC 200 nan-initial #:semantics semantics))
             (⊦ equal?
                (list semantics #t xs)
                (list semantics
                      (construction-check nan-initial #:semantics semantics)
                      (mcmC 200 nan-initial #:semantics semantics #:check #t)))
             (⊦ equal?
                (list semantics '(3))
                (list semantics (delete-duplicates (map car xs))))
             (⊦ equal?
                (list semantics 2)
                (list semantics (length (delete-duplicates (map cdr xs)))))))
    '(upstream extended))
  (for-each
    (λ (semantics)
       (⊦ equal?
          (list semantics #t (mcmC 300 nan-mean #:semantics semantics) 0)
          (list semantics
                (construction-check
                  nan-mean
                  #:seed
                  107336
                  #:semantics
                  semantics)

                                nan-mean
                                #:seed
                                107336
                                #:semantics
                                semantics
                                #:check
                                #t))))))
    '(upstream extended))
  (⊦ equal?
     '()
     (filter-map
       (Λ ((,name unquote m))
          (let1 (residual (kernel-residual m))

test/semantics/check/negative: pass

B4′, the negative controls of B4: each oracle of #:check reports a chain it should reject, with an internal condition. construction-check (plan §6.9, §15 C37) catches a model whose two builds, the initial trace and a rebuild from the same seed, differ in value (a counter answered as the result) or in LL (the same counter as an observation's standard deviation): each run increments the counter after a bind, so each build sees another value. An LL -inf agrees only with -inf: in counter/bound the counter bounds the uniform of an observation in a branch never taken, which is impossible on the first build only, and 'extended mode, which keeps llcum finite, reports it. mcmC runs it once per chain under #:check, and reports both models the same way. A deferred value (deviation 11) is compared by its arguments, since its closure is fresh per build: counter/deferred reads the counter in the inactive branch with a function that raises, and its two builds differ only there. The rebuild also catches a model whose graph alone changes, switching, whose Dirac node reads x on odd runs and y, of the same value, on even ones; and the address-order redraw catches a sampler that draws once more on its third call, the redraw's (third-skips, another value), or that then reports its LL plus 1 (third-shifts, another LL). In 'extended mode the LL that the redraw checks is the extended one, which never counts an inactive observation, so neither does the check's tolerance: the shift is caught next to an inactive observation that is impossible (LL -inf) or far off (LL about -5·10²³). In 'upstream mode, whose LL adds and then subtracts that observation's (M:659), the shift is lost: next to the impossible observation the LL is NaN, which the redraw cannot compare, and next to the far one it is lost in the rounding. A build of LL NaN is still checked, all but the redraw's LL, since initial-step keeps it (it retries only an infinite LL, M:853-854): next to the impossible observation, the redraw of third-skips reports its value.

reference-check catches each of nine corruptions of the initial trace of shifted-normal, x and the Dirac node x + 1, made behind the engine's back with its exported setters, each with its own message: an LL shifted by 1 or made -inf, a value, a distribution or a tstamp after now in a node's state, a deactivation count, a delayed update on an active node, a pending weight left over, and the chain's card change. It also catches, through the wiring of mcmC, a user distribution whose density drifts with the number of its calls. In 'extended mode, where no update is delayed, it checks the inactive nodes' states too: the LL of the inactive branch's choice of tac1, shifted by 1, is reported in an 'extended build and not in an 'upstream one, where an inactive node may hold a stale state.

The step check catches a rejected step that did not restore the state: in behind-the-journal the sampler of the proposed node replaces the state of the observed x by an equal copy with the raw node-state-set!, which no journal entry undoes; the reference evaluator compares values and LLs and accepts the copy, the step check compares the state objects themselves (C5) and does not, in both semantics. Without #:check the chain runs.

In 'extended mode it also catches an accepted step whose Δ is not the change of the extended LL: in tampered the sampler of the only resampleable node, armed before step k, adds 1 to the stored LL of the observed x, which the step's Δ does not count; with no observation of the proposed node every step is accepted. The step check runs before the reference evaluator, which would report the LL instead. #:check 5 checks the first 5 steps only: it reports a corruption at step 5 and runs through one at step 6.

(define (test/semantics/check/negative _)
  (let ((k 0)
        (j 0)
        (u 0)
        (runs 0)
        (calls 0)
        (skips 0)
        (shifts 0)
        (victim #f)
        (shifted #f)
        (tamper! #f))
    (define (drifting)
      (let1 (d (normal 0.0 1.0))
            (make-distribution
              'drifting
              #:log-density
              (λ (x)
                 (add1! calls)
                 (- (distribution-log-density d x) (* 0.001 calls)))
              #:sample
              (λ (src) (distribution-sample d src)))))
    (define (unjournaled)
      (let1 (d (normal 0.0 1.0))
            (make-distribution
              'unjournaled
              #:log-density
              (λ (x) (distribution-log-density d x))
              #:sample
              (λ (src)
                 (when (node? victim)
                       (let1 (s (node-state victim))
                             (node-state-set!
                               victim
                               (make-nstate
                                 (nstate-val s)
                                 (nstate-ll s)
                                 (nstate-dst s)
                                 (nstate-tstamp s)))))
                 (distribution-sample d src)))))
    (define behind-the-journal
      (do/monad
        (← x (dist (condition 0.5 normal) 0.0 1.0))
        (let _ (set! victim x))
        (← s (dist unjournaled))
        (dist (condition 3.0 normal) s 0.5)
        (return s)))
    (define (restate-node! n f)
      (let1 (s (node-state n))
            (node-state-set!
              n
              (f (nstate-val s)
                 (nstate-ll s)
                 (nstate-dst s)
                 (nstate-tstamp s)))))
    (define (restate! ch addr f) (restate-node! (chain-node ch addr) f))
    (define shifted-normal
      (do/monad (← x (dist normal 0.0 1.0)) (diracN (sexp+ x 1.0))))
    (define (corrupted-message
             corrupt!
             #!optional
             (m shifted-normal)
             (semantics 'upstream))
      (internal-message
        (τ (receive
             (ch result)
             (build-chain
               (model->trace m 'test)
               (make-stdgen-source 17)
               semantics)
             (corrupt! ch)
             (reference-check ch)))))
    (define (shift-inactive-ll! ch)
      (restate-node!
        (chain-fold
          (λ (n found)
             (or found
                 (and (node-inactive? n) (eq? (node-kind n) 'resampleable) n)))
          #f
          ch)
        (λ (v ll d t) (make-nstate v (+ 1.0 ll) d t))))
    (define (tampering)
      (let1 (d (normal 0.0 1.0))
            (make-distribution
              'tampering
              #:log-density
              (λ (x) (distribution-log-density d x))
              #:sample
              (λ (src)
                 (when tamper! (let1 (t! tamper!) (set! tamper! #f) (t!)))
                 (distribution-sample d src)))))
    (define tampered
      (do/monad
        (← x (dist (condition 0.5 normal) 0.0 1.0))
        (let _ (set! shifted x))
        (dist tampering)))
    (define counter/k (>>= (return #f) (λ (_) (add1! k) (return k))))
    (define counter/j
      (>>= (return #f)
           (λ (_)
              (add1! j)
              (dist (condition 0.0 normal) 0.0 (exact->inexact j)))))
    (define counter/bound
      (>>= (return #f)
           (λ (_)
              (add1! u)
              (if_ #f
                   (dist (condition 5.0 uniform) 0.0 (if (= u 1) 1.0 10.0))
                   (return 0.0)))))
    (define counter/deferred
      (do/monad
        (← c (dist bern 0.5))
        (>>= (return #f)
             (λ (_)
                (add1! k)
                (if_ #t
                     (return c)
                     (diracN (lift (λ (kv) (car (list-tail '() kv))) k)))))
        (return c)))
    (define switching
      (do/monad
        (← x (diracN 1.0))
        (← y (diracN 1.0))
        (>>= (return #f)
             (λ (_) (add1! runs) (diracN (sexp+ (if (odd? runs) x y) 0.0))))))
    (define (third-skips)
      (let1 (d (normal 0.0 1.0))
            (make-distribution
              'third-skips
              #:log-density
              (λ (x) (distribution-log-density d x))
              #:sample
              (λ (src)
                 (add1! skips)
                 (when (zero? (modulo skips 3)) (distribution-sample d src))
                 (distribution-sample d src)))))
    (define (third-shifts)
      (let1 (d (normal 0.0 1.0))
            (make-distribution
              'third-shifts
              #:log-density
              (λ (x) (distribution-log-density d x))
              #:sample
              (λ (src)
                 (add1! shifts)
                 (receive
                   (x ll)
                   (distribution-sample d src)
                   (values x (if (zero? (modulo shifts 3)) (+ ll 1.0) ll)))))))
    (define (third-shifts-message obs semantics)
      (set! shifts 0)
      (internal-message
        (τ (construction-check
             (do/monad
               (← x (dist third-shifts))
               (if_ #f obs (return 0))
               (return x))
             #:semantics
             semantics))))
    (define (tampered-message check k)
      (set! tamper! #f)
      (internal-message
        (τ (let loop ((s (mcmC§ tampered #:semantics 'extended #:check check))
                      (i 0))
             (car§ s)
             (when (= i (sub1 k))
                   (set! tamper!
                     (τ (restate-node!
                          shifted
                          (λ (v ll d t) (make-nstate v (+ 1.0 ll) d t))))))
             (when (< i 20) (loop (cdr§ s) (add1 i)))))))
    (⊦ equal?
       "a rebuild and the initial trace differ in value"
       (internal-message (τ (construction-check counter/k))))
    (⊦ equal?
       "a rebuild and the initial trace differ in LL"
       (internal-message (τ (construction-check counter/j))))
    (⊦ equal?
       "a rebuild and the initial trace differ in value"
       (internal-message (τ (mcmC 10 counter/k #:check #t))))
    (⊦ equal?
       "a rebuild and the initial trace differ in LL"
       (internal-message (τ (mcmC 10 counter/j #:check #t))))
    (⊦ equal?
       "a rebuild and the initial trace differ in LL"
       (internal-message
         (τ (construction-check counter/bound #:semantics 'extended))))
    (⊦ equal?
       (make-list 3 "a rebuild and the initial trace differ in value")
       (list (internal-message (τ (construction-check counter/deferred)))
             (internal-message
               (τ (construction-check counter/deferred #:semantics 'extended)))
             (internal-message (τ (mcmC 10 counter/deferred #:check #t)))))
    (⊦ equal?
       "a rebuild and the initial trace differ in graph"
       (internal-message (τ (construction-check switching))))
    (⊦ equal?
       "an address-order redraw and the initial trace differ in value"
       (internal-message (τ (construction-check (dist third-skips)))))
    (⊦ equal?
       "an address-order redraw and the initial trace differ in LL"
       (internal-message (τ (construction-check (dist third-shifts)))))
    (let ((impossible (dist (condition 5.0 (uniform 0.0 1.0))))
          (far (dist (condition 1000000000000.0 normal) 0.0 1.0)))
      (⊦ equal?
         (make-list
           2
           "an address-order redraw and the initial trace differ in LL")
         (map (cut third-shifts-message <> 'extended) (list impossible far)))
      (⊦ equal?
         '(none none)
         (map (cut third-shifts-message <> 'upstream) (list impossible far)))
      (set! skips 0)
      (⊦ equal?
         "an address-order redraw and the initial trace differ in value"
         (internal-message
           (τ (construction-check
                (do/monad
                  (← x (dist third-skips))
                  (if_ #f impossible (return 0))
                  (return x)))))))
    (⊦ equal?
       '("an LL differs from its recomputation" none)
       (map (λ (semantics)
               (corrupted-message shift-inactive-ll! tac1 semantics))
            '(extended upstream)))
    (for-each
      (Λ ((,expected ,corrupt!))
         (⊦ equal? expected (corrupted-message corrupt!)))
      `(("an LL differs from its recomputation"
         ,(λ (ch)
             (restate! ch 1 (λ (v ll d t) (make-nstate v (+ 1.0 ll) d t)))))
        ("an LL differs from its recomputation"
         ,(λ (ch) (restate! ch 1 (λ (v ll d t) (make-nstate v -inf.0 d t)))))
        ("a value differs from its recomputation"
         ,(λ (ch) (restate! ch 2 (λ (v ll d t) (make-nstate 99.0 ll d t)))))
        ("a distribution differs from its recomputation"
         ,(λ (ch)
             (restate!
               ch
               1
               (λ (v ll d t)
                  (make-nstate v ll (distk-rd (normal 0.0 2.0)) t)))))
        ("a tstamp is after now"
         ,(λ (ch) (restate! ch 1 (λ (v ll d t) (make-nstate v ll d 99)))))
        ("a deactivation count differs from its recomputation"
         ,(λ (ch) (node-inactive-set! (chain-node ch 1) 1)))
        ("an update is delayed on an active node or in 'extended mode"
         ,(λ (ch) (node-delayed-set! (chain-node ch 1) #t)))
        ("a node is still pending or queued after the step"
         ,(λ (ch) (node-pending-set! (chain-node ch 1) #t)))
        ("the card change differs from its recomputation"
         ,(λ (ch) (chain-card-change-set! ch 1)))))
    (⊦ equal?
       "an LL differs from its recomputation"
       (internal-message (τ (mcmC 50 (dist drifting) #:check #t))))
    (⊦ = 50 (length (mcmC 50 behind-the-journal)))
    (for-each
      (λ (semantics)
         (⊦ equal?
            (list semantics "a rejected step did not restore the state")
            (list semantics
                  (internal-message
                    (τ (mcmC 50
                             behind-the-journal
                             #:check
                             #t
                             #:semantics
                             semantics))))))
      '(upstream extended))
    (⊦ equal?
       "the step's delta is not the extended LL change"
       (tampered-message #t 3))
    (⊦ equal?
       "the step's delta is not the extended LL change"
       (tampered-message 5 5))
    (⊦ eq? 'none (tampered-message 5 6))
    (⊦ eq? 'none (tampered-message #f 3))))

test/semantics/check/escaped-error: pass

B4″: an error that escapes a step leaves its journal open, and the next step first rolls it back (journal-begin!, no upstream counterpart), so a reader that catches the error and reads on continues from the last accepted state. In flaky the update of y raises once, the first time x is proposed true after the build; at seed 3 that is the fifth read of the stream, and #:check #t finds the state sound at every one of the 11 other reads.

(define (test/semantics/check/escaped-error _)
  (let ((armed #f))
    (define flaky
      (do/monad
        (← x (dist bern 0.1))
        (← y
           (dist normal
                 (lift (λ (xv)
                          (if xv
                            (if armed
                              (begin (set! armed #f) (error "flaky"))
                              5.0)
                            0.0))
                       x)
                 1.0))
        (return (pair x y))))
    (let ((s (mcmC§ flaky #:check #t #:seed 3)))
      (set! armed #t)
      (let1 (reads (let loop ((i 0) (acc '()))
                     (if (= i 12)
                       (reverse acc)
                       (loop (add1 i)
                             (cons (condition-case
                                     (let1 (v (car§ s)) (set! s (cdr§ s)) v)
                                     (e (exn)
                                        ((condition-property-accessor 'exn 'message) e)))
                                   acc)))))
            (⊦ equal? '("flaky") (filter string? reads))
            (⊦ = 11 (count pair? reads))))))

((eta 0.001) (memory #(25165824 6214696 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))

test/semantics/invalid-inactive: pass

B5, plan C22: in extended mode an inactive node is updated, so the gamma of a negative shape in the branch not taken takes part in every step. Its sampler raises a parameter error, which rejects the initial trace; its density answers -inf, so an update with x ≤ 0 rejects the step through Δ = -inf. Beta's density raises the parameter error itself (D:330-339): in invalid-inactive/beta the update that raises it rejects the step (plan C22's guarded update) instead of stopping the chain. The extended target is then restricted to the states where every inactive node has valid parameters: here every state with x ≤ 0 holds the inactive gamma x 1, so the chain stays where x > 0 and the result, the gamma sample, is always positive, where the model's posterior has P(x > 0) = 0.5. The extended semantics is opt-in for this reason, among others. In 'upstream mode, at seed 17, the chain stops with the parameter error, raised when the inactive branch is built or updated with x ≤ 0 (at seeds 15 to 19 too; at seed 20 the chain runs, delaying the inactive updates, and gives P(x > 0) = 0.3995); the beta model stops too.

The other side of C22: of the (exn hakaru10) errors only parameter errors reject. Any other (exn hakaru10) error of an inactive node propagates in extended mode, as upstream, at construction (the build guard) and at an update (the guarded update) alike. An error that is not an (exn hakaru10) one, such as car of '() in a lifted function, is one that an inactive Dirac node defers (M:99), and in extended mode it rejects too, raised by the node's own parameters or by forcing a deferred value (case B6). In invalid-inactive/branch the inner test of the else branch is 0, not a boolean, whenever c holds: with p = 0.5 the initial trace at seed 17 has c true and the build raises branch; with p = 0.05 it starts with c false, and the first update that proposes c true raises branch instead of rejecting the step, which would keep the chain at c = #f. In 'upstream mode the build raises too, while the update, which leaves the inactive branch alone, runs the chain through c = #t. The oracles (exact enumeration, likelihood weighting and weighted samples) walk the graph of one 'extended build from seed 17, retried only when it is rejected (plan §15.3, C22), so they share that build's fate: with p = 0.5 they raise branch; with p = 0.05 the build completes, a walk that takes c true skips the inactive inner if_, and enumeration gives P(c) = 0.05.

And of the nodes only the inactive ones reject: an active node's parameter error propagates in 'extended mode as in 'upstream, at construction (a normal of standard deviation 0) and at an update. In active-invalid the observation, always active, gets a beta whose first parameter is -1 when b is false: the initial trace at seed 17 has b true, and the first step that proposes b false raises parameter instead of rejecting.

(define invalid-inactive
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist normal 0.0 1.0))
    (if_ (lift positive? x) (dist gamma x 1.0) (return 0.0))))

(define invalid-inactive/beta
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist normal 0.0 1.0))
    (if_ (lift positive? x) (dist beta x 1.0) (return 0.0))))

(define (invalid-inactive/branch p)
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern p))
    (← x (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ c
         (return 3)
         (if_ (lift (λ (cv xv) (if cv 0 xv)) c x) (return 1) (return 2)))
    (return c)))

(define active-invalid
  (do/monad
    (← b (dist bern 0.9))
    (dist (condition 0.5 beta) (lift (λ (bv) (if bv 1.0 -1.0)) b) 1.0)
    (return b)))

(define (test/semantics/invalid-inactive _)
  (let1 (xs (mcmC 2000 invalid-inactive #:semantics 'extended))
        (⊦ = 2000 (length xs))
        (⊦ every positive? xs))
  (⊦ eq? 'parameter (hakaru10-kind (τ (mcmC 2000 invalid-inactive))))
  (let1 (xs (mcmC 2000 invalid-inactive/beta #:semantics 'extended))
        (⊦ = 2000 (length xs))
        (⊦ every positive? xs))
  (⊦ eq? 'parameter (hakaru10-kind (τ (mcmC 2000 invalid-inactive/beta))))
  (⊦ eq?
     'branch
     (hakaru10-kind
       (τ (mcmC 1 (invalid-inactive/branch 0.5) #:semantics 'extended))))
  (⊦ eq?
     'branch
     (hakaru10-kind
       (τ (mcmC 2000 (invalid-inactive/branch 0.05) #:semantics 'extended))))
  (⊦ eq? 'branch (hakaru10-kind (τ (mcmC 1 (invalid-inactive/branch 0.5)))))
  (let1 (xs (mcmC 2000 (invalid-inactive/branch 0.05)))
        (⊦ = 2000 (length xs))
        (⊦ any identity xs))
  (⊦ equal?
     '(branch branch branch)
     (map (λ (oracle)
             (hakaru10-kind (τ (oracle (invalid-inactive/branch 0.5)))))
          (list exact-posterior
                (cut likelihood-weighting 100 <>)
                (cut weighted-samples 100 <>))))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     '((#f . 0.95) (#t . 0.05))
     (exact-posterior (invalid-inactive/branch 0.05)))
  (⊦ equal?
     '(none none)
     (map (λ (oracle)
             (hakaru10-kind (τ (oracle 100 (invalid-inactive/branch 0.05)))))
          (list likelihood-weighting weighted-samples)))
  (for-each
    (λ (semantics)
       (⊦ equal?
          (list semantics 'parameter 'parameter)
          (list semantics
                (hakaru10-kind
                  (τ (mcmC 10 (dist normal 0.0 0.0) #:semantics semantics)))
                (hakaru10-kind
                  (τ (mcmC 2000 active-invalid #:semantics semantics))))))
    '(extended upstream))
  (⊦ = 1 (length (mcmC 1 active-invalid #:semantics 'extended))))

test/semantics/deferred: pass

B6: deferred Dirac values (M:99, M:120-129). Upstream never forces the value of a Dirac node that nothing reads, so in guarded-car the else branch, inactive exactly when xs is empty, applies car to '() harmlessly. Here the value of an inactive diracN node whose computation raises is a deferred value, which only a strict reader forces. Exact enumeration gives 1/3 to each of a, b and none; 6000 samples of the chain land within .02 of it in both semantics, and #:check finds every state sound; the first five samples at seed 17 are a regression value.

(define guarded-car
  (do/monad
    (← k (dist uniformly '(0 1 2)))
    (← xs (diracN (lift (λ (kv) (list-tail '(a b) kv)) k)))
    (← empty (diracN (lift null? xs)))
    (if_ empty (return 'none) (diracN (lift car xs)))))

In nested-guarded-car the car sits under a second if_, on c: when xs is empty and c holds, the inner if_ is inactive and its exit copies the deferred value without forcing it, which an 'extended step, updating that inactive exit, must do too; forcing it there would reject every step to (#t . none), whose probability is 1/6. The chains of 20000 samples land within .03 of the exact posterior in both semantics, and #:check finds every state sound. The kernel residual of the 'extended semantics (hakaru10-exact.scm, E10) is below 10⁻¹² on both models, and on guarded-car/reversed, whose initial trace has the else branch active (a) where guarded-car's has it inactive (none). The residual enumerates and loads every assignment of the extended space, the else branch's car of '() included, which it must compute as lazily as the chain does, whichever assignment the node was last computed in.

(define nested-guarded-car
  (do/monad
    (← k (dist uniformly '(0 1 2)))
    (← xs (diracN (lift (λ (kv) (list-tail '(a b) kv)) k)))
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← r
       (if_ (lift null? xs)
            (return 'none)
            (if_ c (diracN (lift car xs)) (return 'other))))
    (return (pair c r))))

(define guarded-car/reversed
  (do/monad
    (← k (dist uniformly '(2 1 0)))
    (← xs (diracN (lift (λ (kv) (list-tail '(a b) kv)) k)))
    (← empty (diracN (lift null? xs)))
    (if_ empty (return 'none) (diracN (lift car xs)))))

A handler that a model sets up inside a lifted function, around no model, is an ordinary Scheme handler: the error is raised and caught inside the lifted function, on the values of two choices, and exact enumeration of guarded-inside-lift gives 1/6 to each of its six outcomes.

(define guarded-inside-lift
  (do/monad
    (← b (dist uniformly '(0 1 2)))
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (return
      (lift (λ (bv cv)
               (handle-exceptions
                 e
                 'caught
                 (if (and cv (= bv 0))
                   (error "guarded-inside-lift")
                   (list bv cv))))
            b
            c))))

Deviation 11, strict propagation: strict-reader's mu is the cdr of a pair whose car is the deferred h, so upstream computes mu = 1.0, while here mu is the deferred value itself and the normal, a strict reader, raises car's error, a plain Scheme one, both with a constant test ('()) and with a lifted one; with a non-empty xs the branch is active and nothing is deferred. In 'extended mode the error rejects instead (C22): every build of (strict-reader '()) is rejected, so the chain is empty, and strict-reader/node never takes its then branch.

(define (strict-reader xs)
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ (lift null? xs)
         (return c)
         (do/monad
           (← h (diracN (lift car xs)))
           (← mu (diracN (lift cdr (pair h 1.0))))
           (dist normal mu 1.0)))))

(define strict-reader/node
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (let xs (lift (λ (cv) (if cv '() '(1))) c))
    (if_ (lift null? xs)
         (return 'empty)
         (do/monad
           (← h (diracN (lift car xs)))
           (← mu (diracN (lift cdr (pair h 1.0))))
           (dist normal mu 1.0)))))

A function applied to a deferred value in an inactive branch is not called, even one that ignores it: in ignoring-reader mu is the deferred car of '() itself, so the normal raises in 'upstream mode and rejects every build in 'extended mode, where upstream computes mu = 1.0. And an active diracN computes its value at once: strict-active's then branch, always active, makes a Dirac node of the car of a node whose value is '(), which raises car's error when it is made, in both semantics and under enumeration, where upstream, which never reads that node, never forces it. Lifting too is lazy only in an inactive branch: in strict-active/val the lift of car over the Val '() raises as it is applied.

(define ignoring-reader
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ #t
         (return c)
         (do/monad
           (let d (lift car '()))
           (← mu (diracN (lift (λ (x) 1.0) d)))
           (dist normal mu 1.0)))))

(define strict-active
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← xs (diracN (lift (λ (cv) '()) c)))
    (if_ #t (>> (diracN (lift car xs)) (return c)) (return c))))

(define strict-active/val
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ #t (>> (diracN (lift car '())) (return c)) (return c))))

Lazy lifting does not leak: a model run from inside an inactive branch's lifted function, here an exact-posterior of a model that computes (lift car xs) of an empty xs after a bind, and discards it, starts without it and raises. With lazy lifting leaked, (lift car xs) would be deferred and never forced, and the posterior would be ((leaked . 1.)).

(define (test/semantics/deferred _)
  (let1 (thirds (map (λ (v) (cons v (/ 1.0 3.0))) '(a b none)))
        (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12) thirds (exact-posterior guarded-car))

          (λ (semantics)
             (⊦ (equal-approx? 0.02)
                thirds
                (proportions (mcmC 6000 guarded-car #:semantics semantics)))
             (⊦ equal?
                (mcmC 300 guarded-car #:semantics semantics)
                (mcmC 300 guarded-car #:semantics semantics #:check #t)))
          '(upstream extended)))
  (⊦ equal? '(none none b none a) (mcmC 5 guarded-car))
  (let1 (exact (exact-posterior nested-guarded-car))
        (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
           (map cons
                '((#f . none) (#f . other) (#t . a) (#t . b) (#t . none))
                (map (λ (w) (/ w 6.0)) '(1.0 2.0 1.0 1.0 1.0)))
           exact)
        (for-each
          (λ (semantics)
             (⊦ (equal-approx? 0.03)
                (list semantics exact)
                (list semantics
                      (proportions
                        (mcmC 20000
                              nested-guarded-car
                              #:semantics
                              semantics))))
             (⊦ equal?
                (mcmC 300 nested-guarded-car #:semantics semantics)
                (mcmC 300
                      nested-guarded-car
                      #:semantics
                      semantics
                      #:check
                      #t)))
          '(upstream extended)))
  (⊦ equal?
     '()
     (filter-map
       (Λ ((,name unquote m))
          (let1 (residual (kernel-residual m))
                (and (not (< residual 1e-12)) (cons name residual))))
       `((guarded-car unquote guarded-car)
         (guarded-car/reversed unquote guarded-car/reversed)
         (nested-guarded-car unquote nested-guarded-car))))
  (⊦ equal?
     '(a none)
     (list (car (mcmC 1 guarded-car/reversed)) (car (mcmC 1 guarded-car))))
  (let1 (sixths
          (map (λ (v) (cons v (/ 1.0 6.0)))
               '(caught (0 #f) (1 #f) (1 #t) (2 #f) (2 #t))))
        (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12) sixths (exact-posterior guarded-inside-lift)))
  (⊦ equal?
     '(exn none exn)
     (map (λ (m) (raised (τ (mcmC 3 m))))
          (list (strict-reader '()) (strict-reader '(5)) strict-reader/node)))
  (⊦ equal? '() (mcmC 3 (strict-reader '()) #:semantics 'extended))
  (⊦ every flonum? (mcmC 300 strict-reader/node #:semantics 'extended))
  (for-each
    (λ (m)
       (⊦ equal?
          '(exn exn exn)
          (list (raised (τ (mcmC 3 m)))
                (raised (τ (mcmC 3 m #:semantics 'extended)))
                (raised (τ (exact-posterior m))))))
    (list strict-active strict-active/val))
  (⊦ equal?
     '(exn ())
     (list (raised (τ (mcmC 3 ignoring-reader)))
           (mcmC 3 ignoring-reader #:semantics 'extended)))
  (let1 (inner #f)
        (mcmC 1
              (do/monad
                (← c (dist bern 0.5))
                (if_ #t
                     (return c)
                     (diracN
                       (lift (λ (k)
                                (set! inner
                                  (raised
                                    (τ (exact-posterior
                                         (do/monad
                                           (← xs (return '()))
                                           (let _ (lift car xs))
                                           (return 'leaked))))))
                                k)
                             1)))))
        (⊦ eq? 'exn inner)))

test/semantics/incremental: pass

B8: incremental recomputation (P §4.2 l.765-800). Every node's update is wrapped to record, step by step, which updates ran and in which order. In pdep resampling x updates y (whose LL changes, not its value) and the Dirac result, never z; resampling y updates z and the result; resampling z the result alone:

❝
Suppose MH proposes to resample x (and only x). Although the y sample keeps its old value, its distribution parameters, x specifically, changed. Therefore, the LL of the old y sample in the new distribution has to be recomputed. The variable z is not affected by the resampling proposal; also, the parameters of its distribution remain the same. Therefore, no update to the z node is needed.

Oleg Kiselyov

(define pdep
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist normal 0.0 1.0))
    (← y (dist normal x 1.0))
    (← z (dist normal y 1.0))
    (return (sexp+ x y z))))

(define (update-traces n model . keys)
  (let ((updated '()) (steps '()))
    (define (spy! ch)
      (chain-fold
        (λ (node _)
           (let1 (update (node-update node))
                 (when update
                       (node-update-set!
                         node
                         (λ (n) (push! (node-addr n) updated) (update n))))))
        (void)
        ch))
    (define (collect! ch accepted? delta)
      (push! (reverse updated) steps)
      (set! updated '()))
    (let1 (next (apply mcmc-generator
                       n
                       (model->trace model 'update-traces)
                       #:build-hook
                       spy!
                       #:step-hook
                       collect!
                       keys))
          (do ((i 0 (add1 i))) ((= i n)) (next)))
    (reverse steps)))

The propagation-order note of Metropolis.hs (M:319-397): in upp1 and upp2, d depends on a, b and c, and resampling a updates b, c and d, each once, in this order, d last; resampling d updates nothing. Both models also run under #:check #t, whose reference evaluator recomputes every node after each step, in both semantics.

(define upp1
  (do/monad
    (← a (dist uniform 0.0 1.0))
    (← b (diracN (sexp+ a 2.0)))
    (← c (diracN (sexp+ a 3.0)))
    (← d (dist uniform (sexp+ a b) c))
    (return d)))

(define upp2
  (do/monad
    (← a (dist uniform 0.0 1.0))
    (← b (diracN (sexp+ a 2.0)))
    (← c (diracN (sexp+ b 3.0)))
    (← d (dist uniform (sexp+ a b) c))
    (return d)))

Upstream runs upp1 as M:336-341 writes it, on the raw trace API, where d depends on c and on an extra Dirac node ab = a + b: resampling a updates b, c, ab and d, each once, in this order.

(define upp1/raw
  (do/monad
    (← a (with-node-none (uniform 0.0 1.0)))
    (← b (with-node a (λ (av) (dirac (+ av 2.0)))))
    (← c (with-node a (λ (av) (dirac (+ av 3.0)))))
    (← ab
       (with-node-tree
         (vector a b)
         (λ (vs) (dirac (+ (vector-ref vs 0) (vector-ref vs 1))))))
    (with-node-tree
      (vector ab c)
      (λ (vs) (uniform (vector-ref vs 0) (vector-ref vs 1))))))

(define (test/semantics/incremental _)
  (for-each
    (λ (semantics)
       (⊦ equal?
          (list semantics '((2 4) (3 4) (4)))
          (list semantics
                (map car
                     (hist (update-traces 1000 pdep #:semantics semantics)))))
       (for-each
         (λ (m)
            (⊦ equal?
               (list semantics '(() (2 3 4)))
               (list semantics
                     (map car
                          (hist (update-traces 1000 m #:semantics semantics)))))
            (⊦ = 300 (length (mcmC 300 m #:semantics semantics #:check #t))))
         (list upp1 upp2))
       (⊦ equal?
          (list semantics '(() (2 3 4 5)))
          (list semantics
                (map car
                     (hist (update-traces
                             1000
                             upp1/raw
                             #:semantics
                             semantics)))))
       (⊦ = 300 (length (mcmC 300 upp1/raw #:semantics semantics #:check #t))))
    '(upstream extended)))

(aux hakaru10): posterior estimates within statistical tolerances

Statistics

Posterior estimates of the examples, checked against exact or analytic values. Each tolerance is at least about 4 standard deviations of the estimate across the seeds 1 to 40, measured on this port, and more where a looser plan value is kept (the chains are autocorrelated, so the iid standard error underestimates the spread two- to fourfold); the averages over those seeds agree with the exact values, with no sign of bias. The suite runs at seed 17 by default; HAKARU10_SEEDS=17,1,2,3,4 (make test-hakaru10-long) runs every case over the listed seeds, a failure reporting its seed. Seeds this run: (17).

test/statistics/phier-kl: pass

S5: phier, Hur et al.'s Fig. 3, has the distribution N(0, √91) (P l.313-318), on which STAN goes wrong. The KL divergence of 40000 samples, tabulated in bins of 0.5, from the exact bin masses is below 0.25 (over the seeds 1 to 40: mean 0.080, standard deviation 0.038, at most 0.207; at 10000 samples the mean is 0.29 but the spread reaches 0.98, too wide for a test). Upstream's estimates, sample against sample (E:76-85):

phier_kl n =
  kl (tabulate 0.5 (mcmC n phier))
     (tabulate 0.5 (mcmC n (dist normal 0 (sqrt <$> 91))))
     

_ =  phier_kl 10

test/statistics/impossible-branches: pass

S12: the else branches observe values outside the uniform's support (log likelihood -inf): an initial trace taking them is built again (M:882-891), and no step can switch to them (rejectedLL, M:853-854, M:927). Exact enumeration prunes the paths of weight 0, so the three posteriors are exactly ((#t . 1.)).

(define (prog-mixture1 cv)
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ c
         (dist (condition cv normal) 1.0 1.0)
         (dist (condition cv uniform) 0.0 3.0))
    (return c)))

(define tac10
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ c
         (dist (condition 0.0 normal) 0.0 1.0)
         (dist (condition 0.0 uniform) 10.0 20.0))
    (return c)))

(define tac11
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ c
         (dist (condition 0.0 normal) 0.0 1.0)
         (do/monad (← d (diracN 10.0)) (dist (condition 0.0 uniform) d 20.0)))
    (return c)))

prog_mixture1 cv = do
  c <- dist bern 0.5
  if_ c (dist (cv `condition` normal)  1 1)
        (dist (cv `condition` uniform) 0 3)
  return c

prog_mixture1_run = mcmC 20 (prog_mixture1 (-2))
-- all true
tac10 = do
  c <- dist bern 0.5
  if_ c
    -- then
    (dist (0 `condition` normal) 0 1)
    -- else
    (dist (0 `condition` uniform) 10 20)
  return c

tac10_run = length . filter id $ mcmC 100 tac10
-- 100  -- all True

tac11 = do
  c <- dist bern 0.5
  if_ c
    -- then
    (dist (0 `condition` normal) 0 1)
    -- else
    (do
        d <- diracN 10
        dist (0 `condition` uniform) d 20)
  return c

tac11_run = length . filter id $ mcmC 100 tac11
-- 100  -- all True
(define (test/statistics/impossible-branches _)
  (for-each
    (λ (seed)
       (⊦ equal?
          (list seed (make-list 100 #t))
          (list seed (mcmC 100 (prog-mixture1 -2.0) #:seed seed)))
       (⊦ equal?
          (list seed (make-list 100 #t))
          (list seed (mcmC 100 tac10 #:seed seed)))
       (⊦ equal?
          (list seed (make-list 100 #t))
          (list seed (mcmC 100 tac11 #:seed seed))))
    seeds)
  (⊦ equal?
     (make-list 3 '((#t . 1.0)))
     (map exact-posterior (list (prog-mixture1 -2.0) tac10 tac11))))

S14: exif1 is 2d with d ~ N(1, 1) when c holds, else 5 + N(20, 1): mean 13.5, half above 10; exif2 is 10 or 20 a quarter of the time each, else the sum of two uniforms (S:314-337).

(define exif1
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← d
       (if_ c
            (dist normal (lift (λ (cv) (if cv 1.0 0.0)) c) 1.0)
            (diracN 5.0)))
    (← e (if_ (lift not c) (dist normal 20.0 1.0) (diracN d)))
    (diracN (sexp+ d e))))

(define exif2
  (let ()
    (define (cl1 x) (do/monad (← d (dist bern 0.5)) (if_ d cl3 (diracN x))))
    (define cl3
      (do/monad
        (← a (dist uniform 0.0 1.0))
        (← b (dist uniform 0.0 1.0))
        (diracN (sexp+ a b))))
    (do/monad (← c (dist bern 0.5)) (if_ c (cl1 10.0) (cl1 20.0)))))

exif1 = do
  c <- dist bern 0.5
  d <- if_ c (dist normal ((fromIntegral . fromEnum) <$> c) 1)
             (diracN 5)
  e <- if_ (not <$> c) (dist normal 20 1) (diracN d)
  diracN (d + e)

exif1r = mcmC 7 exif1

exif2 = do
  c <- dist bern 0.5
  if_ c (cl1 10) (cl1 20)
 where
   cl1 x = do
     d <- dist bern 0.5
     if_ d cl3 (diracN x)
     
   cl3 = do
     a <- dist uniform 0 1
     b <- dist uniform 0 1
     diracN (a + b)
     

exif2r = mcmC 17 exif2

(define (phierN′ n start obs)
  (fold/monad
    (λ (x k) (k x))
    start
    (append
      (make-list (sub1 n) (λ (x) (dist normal x 3.0)))
      (list (λ (x) (dist (condition obs normal) x 3.0))))))

(define condhier
  (do/monad (← x (dist normal 0.0 1.0)) (phierN′ 9 x 1.0) (return x)))

phierN' :: Int -> SExp Double -> Double -> Model Double
phierN' n start obs =
  foldl (>>=) (pure start) $
  replicate (n-1) (\x -> dist normal x 3) ++
  [(\x -> dist (obs `condition` normal) x 3)]

condhier = do
  x <- dist normal 0 1
  phierN' 9 x 1
  return x
(define (test/statistics/condhier _)
