(aux hakaru10 exact): exact posteriors of finite models

Exact oracles

Hakaru10 infers by Markov chain Monte Carlo, so its answers are estimates. To test them we need exact answers, and oracles that share as little code as possible with the engine they check. This page is about the oracles of (aux hakaru10 exact): exact enumeration, a reference evaluator, and the exact kernel of the 'extended semantics. None of them is in upstream: they are new in the port.

A model is a value of the State monad that threads the chain (Syntax.hs:82), written with do/monad: dist (an observation is the dist of a conditioned distribution), diracN and if_ are models, and running the model creates upstream's trace graph, node by node in bind order. The oracles do not run a model in a semantics of their own: they build it once, in 'extended mode, and walk the graph of that build (plan §6.10, C37). One build is the whole graph, because SExp is applicative, not a monad (Syntax.hs:118), and if_ builds both of its branches (Metropolis.hs:650-661). Exact enumeration visits the nodes in address order, which is their creation order, and builds a lazy search tree in the format of Embedded probabilistic domain-specific language HANSEI:

type 'a vc = V of 'a | C of (unit -> 'a pV) and 'a pV = (prob * 'a vc) list

in Scheme the list (((V v) p) ...) and (((C thunk) p) ...). A choice becomes one C entry per support point (x . p) with p > 0, whose thunk continues the walk with x. An observation becomes one entry weighted by its likelihood, or no entry at all when that likelihood is 0 or its LL is not finite (NaN, +inf; E6). The nodes of a branch that the values on the path leave inactive are skipped. A thunk may be forced in any order, so it first restores the node states of its own path. probcc-explore and probcc-normalize of (aux hansei) then explore and normalize the tree. Only Hansei's data-level tools are used: no oracle uses delimited control.

Enumeration and forward sampling share the graph and the node functions with the engine, but none of its incremental machinery: propagation, the update queue, the switch of branches, the delayed and pending updates, the journal and the acceptance ratio. So an exact posterior against an MH chain tests the kernel and the incremental update. The build checks the static discipline as it goes, such as observations external to the model or no node escaping its branch (H5). Continuous observations weigh by their density, which is right for posteriors over discrete choices. A continuous choice on a path the enumeration takes is a support error, and so are more paths than #:max-paths.

A model runs several times: once per oracle call, and once more for the rebuild of construction-check. So a Scheme effect that must happen once per run goes inside a bind (plan §15, C35). Pre-allocation with replicate/monad (H8), folds and traversals are all safe. One build stands for every build only if the model does not branch in plain Scheme on a node or on a value drawn while it is built. Nothing enforces that, and nothing catches a model that breaks it: the rebuild of construction-check (H2) draws the same values from a copy of the same source, so it takes the same branch. The rebuild catches only a construction that depends on state outliving a run, such as a counter (hakaru10.scm, case B4′).

The kernel residual (E10) checks the MH kernel itself, not one of its chains. In 'extended mode the inactive resampleable nodes are auxiliary variables with their prior. Over every assignment x of all resampleable nodes, inactive ones included, the target is π(x) ∝ exp L(x), where L sums the LLs of every resampleable node and of every active observed node. The one-step kernel T is computed exactly from the engine's own propagation and acceptance code: each active resampleable node is picked with probability 1/N(x), each support point v of its distribution is proposed with its probability q, and the forced step is accepted with probability min(1, eᵅ) and then rolled back. The residual is maxₓ |(πT)(x) − π(x)|, which is below 10⁻¹² when π is stationary for T. This is upstream's merged-node argument made exact (Metropolis.hs:1145-1168):

So, when it comes to computing the acceptance ratio for a program
with branches, we take

p4 = do
     c <- bernN (Val 0.5)
     if_ c (do e1 e2 ...)
           (do e3 e4 e5 ...)

to be

p4' = do
      c <- bernN (Val 0.5)
      if_ c e_then e_else

That is, we merge (at least for the purpose of presentation) e1, e2 ...
into one node e_then, whose ll is the sum of ll of constituents. Ditto
for e_else.

When we switch the branches, we remove the ll of one branch from
llcum and add ll of the other branch. In our terms, we remove the ll
of the deactivated nodes and add ll of the activated nodes.

This algorithm is resilient with respect to adding replacing a node e
with e >>= diracN -- the desired behavior.

In 'upstream mode the argument does not hold for resampleable nodes whose parameters change while they are inactive, as pswitch shows (E8; the main suite's hakaru10-semantics). 'extended mode is the fix, and E10 shows that it leaves the target invariant.

(module
  (aux hakaru10 exact)
  *
  (import
    scheme
    (chicken base)
    (only (chicken memory representation) record-instance? record->vector)
    (only srfi-1 filter-map count fold iota any)
    srfi-69
    (aux base)
    (only (aux hansei) probcc-τ probcc-unit probcc-explore probcc-normalize)
    (only (aux hakaru10 random) make-stdgen-source rng-source-copy)
    (aux hakaru10 distribution)
    (only (aux hakaru10 util) histogram-sort)
    (aux hakaru10 engine))
  (define (ll-close? a b #!optional (scale (abs a)))
    (or (= a b)
        (and (nan? a) (nan? b))
        (and (finite? a)
             (finite? b)
             (<= (abs (- a b)) (* 1e-09 (max 1.0 scale))))))
  (define (same-value? a b)
    (let1 (budget 100000)
          (define (spent?) (set! budget (sub1 budget)) (negative? budget))
          (let walk ((a a) (b b))
            (cond ((eq? a b) #t)
                  ((and (flonum? a) (flonum? b))
                   (or (eqv? a b) (and (nan? a) (nan? b))))
                  ((and (procedure? a) (procedure? b)) #t)
                  ((and (promise? a) (promise? b)) #t)
                  ((and (pair? a) (pair? b))
                   (or (spent?)
                       (and (walk (car a) (car b)) (walk (cdr a) (cdr b)))))
                  ((and (vector? a) (vector? b))
                   (and (= (vector-length a) (vector-length b))
                        (let loop ((i 0))
                          (or (= i (vector-length a))
                              (spent?)
                              (and (walk (vector-ref a i) (vector-ref b i))
                                   (loop (add1 i)))))))
                  ((or (deferred? a) (deferred? b)) (equal? a b))
                  ((and (record-instance? a) (record-instance? b))
                   (walk (record->vector a) (record->vector b)))
                  (else (equal? a b))))))
  (define (choice-support dk who)
    (or (distribution-support dk)
        (hakaru10-error
          'support
          who
          "a random choice without a finite support"
          dk)))
  (define (build model src semantics who)
    (build-chain (model->trace model who) src semantics))
  (define (static-graph model who)
    (let1 (src (make-stdgen-source 17))
          (let retry ((builds 1000))
            (if (zero? builds)
              (hakaru10-error
                'construction
                who
                "no complete build of the model in 1000 tries"
                model)
              (receive
                (ch result)
                (build model src 'extended who)
                (if result (values ch result) (retry (sub1 builds))))))))
  (define (walk-active! n)
    (let1 (k (scope-inactivity n)) (node-inactive-set! n k) (zero? k)))
  (define (branch-starts ch)
    (let1 (starts (make-vector (add1 (chain-count ch)) '()))
          (chain-fold
            (λ (n _)
               (when (eq? (node-role n) 'exit)
                     (let1 (b (node-branch n))
                           (for-each
                             (Λ ((,lo unquote hi) ,polarity)
                                (when (< lo hi)
                                      (vector-set!
                                        starts
                                        (add1 lo)
                                        (cons (list hi (branch-entry b) polarity)
                                              (vector-ref starts (add1 lo))))))
                             (list (branch-then-range b) (branch-else-range b))
                             '(#t #f)))))
            (void)
            ch)
          starts))
  (define (past-inactive-branches starts a)
    (let1 (hi (and (< a (vector-length starts))
                   (any (Λ ((,hi ,entry ,polarity))
                           (and (not (eq? (node-value entry) polarity)) hi))
                        (vector-ref starts a))))
          (if hi (past-inactive-branches starts (add1 hi)) a)))
  (define (graph->search-tree
           ch
           result
           #!key
           (max-paths #f)
           (who 'exact-posterior))
    (let ((last (chain-count ch))
          (now (chain-now ch))
          (starts (branch-starts ch))
          (paths 0))
      (define (restore! frame)
        (for-each
          (Λ ((,n unquote s)) (node-inactive-set! n 0) (node-state-set! n s))
          frame))
      (let walk ((a (past-inactive-branches starts 1)) (frame '()))
        (define (enter n s)
          (node-state-set! n s)
          (walk (past-inactive-branches starts (add1 a))
                (cons (cons n s) frame)))
        (if (> a last)
          (begin
            (add1! paths)
            (when (and max-paths (> paths max-paths))
                  (hakaru10-error
                    'support
                    who
                    "more paths than max-paths"
                    max-paths))
            (probcc-unit (force-value (node-value result))))
          (let1 (n (chain-node ch a))
                (cond ((not (walk-active! n))
                       (walk (past-inactive-branches starts (add1 a)) frame))
                      ((resampleable? n)
                       (filter-map
                         (Λ ((,x unquote p))
                            (and (positive? p)
                                 (probcc-τ
                                   p
                                   (restore! frame)
                                   (enter n (recomputed-state n now x)))))
                         (choice-support (node-distk n) who)))
                      ((eq? (node-kind n) 'observed)
                       (let* ((s (recomputed-state n now))
                              (w (exp (nstate-ll s))))
                         (if (or (not (finite? (nstate-ll s))) (zero? w))
                           '()
                           (list (probcc-τ w (restore! frame) (enter n s))))))
                      (else (enter n (recomputed-state n now)))))))))
  (define (model->search-tree model #!key (max-paths #f))
    (receive
      (ch result)
      (static-graph model 'model->search-tree)
      (graph->search-tree
        ch
        result
        #:max-paths
        max-paths
        #:who
        'model->search-tree)))
  (define (exact-posterior model #!key (max-paths #f))
    (receive
      (ch result)
      (static-graph model 'exact-posterior)
      (histogram-sort
        (map (Λ (((V ,v) ,p)) (cons v p))
             (probcc-normalize
               (probcc-explore
                 +inf.0
                 (graph->search-tree ch result #:max-paths max-paths)))))))
  (define (graph-forward ch result src #!optional (starts (branch-starts ch)))
    (let ((last (chain-count ch)) (now (chain-now ch)))
      (let walk ((a (past-inactive-branches starts 1)) (ll 0.0))
        (define (next) (past-inactive-branches starts (add1 a)))
        (if (> a last)
          (values (force-value (node-value result)) ll)
          (let1 (n (chain-node ch a))
                (cond ((not (walk-active! n)) (walk (next) ll))
                      ((memq (node-kind n) '(resampleable observed))
                       (receive
                         (x llx)
                         (distk-sample (node-distk n) src)
                         (node-state-set! n (recomputed-state n now x))
                         (walk (next) (if (resampleable? n) ll (+ ll llx)))))
                      (else
                       (node-state-set! n (recomputed-state n now))
                       (walk (next) ll))))))))
  (define (weighted-samples n model #!key (seed 17) (rng #f))
    (receive
      (ch result)
      (static-graph model 'weighted-samples)
      (let ((src (or rng (make-stdgen-source seed)))
            (starts (branch-starts ch)))
        (let loop ((i 0) (samples '()))
          (if (>= i n)
            (reverse samples)
            (receive
              (v ll)
              (graph-forward ch result src starts)
              (loop (add1 i) (cons (cons v ll) samples))))))))
  (define (likelihood-weighting n model #!key (seed 17) (rng #f))
    (let* ((samples
             (filter-map
               (Λ ((,v unquote ll)) (and (finite? ll) (cons v ll)))
               (weighted-samples n model #:seed seed #:rng rng)))
           (top (fold (λ (s m) (max (cdr s) m)) -inf.0 samples))
           (table (make-hash-table equal?)))
      (for-each
        (Λ ((,v unquote ll))
           (hash-table-update!/default
             table
             v
             (λ (w) (+ w (exp (- ll top))))
             0.0))
        samples)
      (let1 (total (hash-table-fold table (λ (v w acc) (+ acc w)) 0.0))
            (histogram-sort
              (map (Λ ((,v unquote w)) (cons v (/ w total)))
                   (hash-table->alist table))))))
  (define (node-signature n)
    (list (node-addr n)
          (node-kind n)
          (node-role n)
          (map node-addr (vector->list (node-parents n)))
          (map (Λ ((,entry unquote polarity))
                  (cons (node-addr entry) polarity))
               (node-scope n))))
  (define (same-built-value? a b)
    (cond ((and (deferred? a) (deferred? b))
           (same-value? (deferred-args a) (deferred-args b)))
          ((or (deferred? a) (deferred? b)) #f)
          (else (same-value? a b))))
  (define (first-mismatch same? ch ch*)
    (let loop ((a 1))
      (and (<= a (chain-count ch))
           (if (same? (chain-node ch a) (chain-node ch* a))
             (loop (add1 a))
             a))))
  (define (construction-check
           model
           #!key
           (seed 17)
           (rng #f)
           (semantics 'upstream))
    (let1 (src (or rng (make-stdgen-source seed)))
          (receive
            (ch result)
            (build model (rng-source-copy src) semantics 'check)
            (if (or (not result) (rejected-ll? (chain-llcum ch)))
              'skipped
              (receive
                (ch* result*)
                (build model (rng-source-copy src) semantics 'check)
                (rebuild-check ch result ch* result*)
                (redraw-check ch (rng-source-copy src))
                #t)))))
  (define (rebuild-check ch result ch* result*)
    (define (differ what . args)
      (apply hakaru10-error
             'internal
             'check
             (string-append "a rebuild and the initial trace differ in " what)
             args))
    (unless
      (and result*
           (= (chain-count ch) (chain-count ch*))
           (= (node-addr result) (node-addr result*)))
      (differ "graph" (chain-count ch) (and result* (chain-count ch*))))
    (cond ((first-mismatch
             (λ (n n*) (equal? (node-signature n) (node-signature n*)))
             ch
             ch*)
           =>
           (λ (a) (differ "graph" a)))
          ((first-mismatch
             (λ (n n*) (same-built-value? (node-value n) (node-value n*)))
             ch
             ch*)
           =>
           (λ (a)
              (differ
                "value"
                a
                (node-value (chain-node ch a))
                (node-value (chain-node ch* a)))))
          ((first-mismatch
             (λ (n n*)
                (ll-close?
                  (nstate-ll (node-state n))
                  (nstate-ll (node-state n*))))
             ch
             ch*)
           =>
           (λ (a) (differ "LL" a)))
          ((not (ll-close? (chain-llcum ch) (chain-llcum ch*)))
           (differ "LL" (chain-llcum ch) (chain-llcum ch*)))))
  (define (redraw-check ch src)
    (define (differ what . args)
      (apply hakaru10-error
             'internal
             'check
             (string-append
               "an address-order redraw and the initial trace differ in "
               what)
             args))
    (match/first
      (chain-fold
        (Λ (,n (,ll unquote scale))
           (cond ((resampleable? n)
                  (receive
                    (x llx)
                    (distk-sample (node-distk n) src)
                    (unless
                      (same-value? x (node-value n))
                      (differ "value" (node-addr n) x (node-value n)))
                    (cons (+ ll llx) (+ scale (abs llx)))))
                 ((eq? (node-kind n) 'observed)
                  (let ((llo (nstate-ll (node-state n)))
                        (inactive? (node-inactive? n)))
                    (cons (if inactive? ll (+ ll llo))
                          (if (and inactive? (extended? ch))
                            scale
                            (+ scale (abs llo))))))
                 (else (cons ll scale))))
        '(0.0 . 0.0)
        ch)
      ((,ll unquote scale)
       (let1 (llcum (chain-llcum ch))
             (unless
               (or (nan? llcum) (ll-close? ll llcum scale))
               (differ "LL" ll llcum))))))
  (define (resampleable? n) (eq? (node-kind n) 'resampleable))
  (define (node-distk n)
    (let1 (fn (node-fn n))
          (if (distk? fn) fn (fn (leaf-values (node-parents n))))))
  (define (recomputed-state n ts #!optional (v (node-value n)))
    (if (eq? (node-role n) 'exit)
      (make-nstate
        (let1 (v (node-value (branch-active-result (node-branch n))))
              (if (node-inactive? n) v (force-value v)))
        0.0
        #f
        ts)
      (let* ((dk (node-distk n)) (kind (distk-kind dk)) (rd (distk-rd dk)))
        (unless
          (eq? kind (node-kind n))
          (hakaru10-error
            'construction
            'check
            "a distribution constructor changed the kind of its node"
            n
            (node-kind n)
            kind))
        (case kind
          ((dirac)
           (make-nstate
             (let1 (x (distk-value dk))
                   (if (node-inactive? n) x (force-value x)))
             0.0
             #f
             ts))
          ((observed)
           (let1 (obs (distk-value dk))
                 (check-observation n obs)
                 (make-nstate obs ((rdistr-log-density rd) obs) rd ts)))
          (else (make-nstate v ((rdistr-log-density rd) v) rd ts))))))
  (define (scope-inactivity n)
    (count (Λ ((,entry unquote polarity))
              (not (eq? (node-value entry) polarity)))
           (node-scope n)))
  (define (same-rdistr? a b)
    (if (and a b)
      (and (eq? (rdistr-name a) (rdistr-name b))
           (same-value? (rdistr-params a) (rdistr-params b)))
      (eq? a b)))
  (define (reference-check ch)
    (define (fail msg n . args)
      (apply hakaru10-error 'internal 'check msg (node-addr n) args))
    (define (check-state n s)
      (let1 (s* (recomputed-state n (nstate-tstamp s) (nstate-val s)))
            (unless
              (same-value? (nstate-val s) (nstate-val s*))
              (fail "a value differs from its recomputation"
                    n
                    (nstate-val s)
                    (nstate-val s*)))
            (unless
              (same-rdistr? (nstate-dst s) (nstate-dst s*))
              (fail "a distribution differs from its recomputation"
                    n
                    (nstate-dst s)
                    (nstate-dst s*)))
            (unless
              (ll-close? (nstate-ll s) (nstate-ll s*))
              (fail "an LL differs from its recomputation"
                    n
                    (nstate-ll s)
                    (nstate-ll s*)))))
    (let ((now (chain-now ch)) (every-node? (extended? ch)))
      (chain-fold
        (λ (n _)
           (let ((s (node-state n)) (inactive (node-inactive n)))
             (unless
               (= inactive (scope-inactivity n))
               (fail "a deactivation count differs from its recomputation"
                     n
                     inactive
                     (scope-inactivity n)))
             (when (and (node-delayed n) (or every-node? (zero? inactive)))
                   (fail "an update is delayed on an active node or in 'extended mode"
                         n))
             (when (or (node-pending n) (node-queued n))
                   (fail "a node is still pending or queued after the step" n))
             (when (> (nstate-tstamp s) now)
                   (fail "a tstamp is after now" n (nstate-tstamp s) now))
             (when (or every-node? (zero? inactive)) (check-state n s))))
        (void)
        ch)
      (let1 (card (- (count node-inactive? (chain-sampleable ch))))
            (unless
              (= (chain-card-change ch) card)
              (hakaru10-error
                'internal
                'check
                "the card change differs from its recomputation"
                (chain-card-change ch)
                card)))
      #t))
  (define (resampleable-nodes ch) (reverse (chain-sampleable ch)))
  (define (load-state! ch xs)
    (let* ((now (chain-now ch))
           (rest (chain-fold
                   (λ (n xs)
                      (node-inactive-set! n (scope-inactivity n))
                      (node-state-set!
                        n
                        (if (resampleable? n)
                          (if (pair? xs)
                            (recomputed-state n now (car xs))
                            (hakaru10-error
                              'construction
                              'load-state!
                              "too few values"
                              n))
                          (recomputed-state n now)))
                      (node-delayed-set! n #f)
                      (node-pending-set! n #f)
                      (if (resampleable? n) (cdr xs) xs))
                   xs
                   ch)))
      (unless
        (null? rest)
        (hakaru10-error 'construction 'load-state! "too many values" rest))
      (chain-card-change-set!
        ch
        (- (count node-inactive? (chain-sampleable ch))))))
  (define (ext-states ch #!key (max-states 100000))
    (let ((now (chain-now ch)) (last (chain-count ch)) (found 0))
      (reverse
        (let walk ((a 1) (prefix '()) (states '()))
          (if (> a last)
            (begin
              (add1! found)
              (when (> found max-states)
                    (hakaru10-error
                      'support
                      'ext-states
                      "more states than max-states"
                      max-states))
              (cons (reverse prefix) states))
            (let1 (n (chain-node ch a))
                  (node-inactive-set! n (scope-inactivity n))
                  (if (resampleable? n)
                    (fold (Λ ((,x unquote p) ,states)
                             (if (positive? p)
                               (begin
                                 (node-state-set! n (recomputed-state n now x))
                                 (walk (add1 a) (cons x prefix) states))
                               states))
                          states
                          (choice-support (node-distk n) 'ext-states))
                    (begin
                      (node-state-set! n (recomputed-state n now))
                      (walk (add1 a) prefix states)))))))))
  (define (forced-step-log-alpha ch n x)
    (when (or (not (resampleable? n)) (node-inactive? n))
          (hakaru10-error
            'internal
            'forced-step-log-alpha
            "an active resampleable node is expected"
            n))
    (journal-begin! ch)
    (chain-now-set! ch (add1 (chain-now ch)))
    (chain-llcum-set! ch 0.0)
    (let* ((card-old (chain-card-change ch))
           (s (node-state n))
           (ll ((rdistr-log-density (nstate-dst s)) x))
           (fb (apply-proposal! n x ll (- ll (nstate-ll s))))
           (delta (begin (propagate! ch) (chain-llcum ch)))
           (ratio (if (rejected-ll? delta)
                    -inf.0
                    (log-acceptance-ratio
                      ch
                      (vector-length (chain-samp ch))
                      card-old
                      fb))))
      (journal-rollback! ch)
      ratio))
  (define (kernel-residual
           model
           #!key
           (seed 17)
           (max-states 100000)
           (semantics 'extended))
    (receive
      (ch result)
      (build model (make-stdgen-source seed) semantics 'kernel-residual)
      (unless
        result
        (hakaru10-error
          'construction
          'kernel-residual
          "the model's first build was rejected"))
      (let* ((nodes (resampleable-nodes ch))
             (states (ext-states ch #:max-states max-states))
             (log-weights
               (map (λ (xs) (load-state! ch xs) (chain-extended-ll ch))
                    states))
             (top (let1 (top (fold (λ (l top) (if (finite? l) (max l top) top))
                                   -inf.0
                                   log-weights))
                        (if (finite? top)
                          top
                          (hakaru10-error
                            'construction
                            'kernel-residual
                            "no state of the model has a positive finite weight"
                            top))))
             (weights
               (map (λ (l) (if (finite? l) (exp (- l top)) 0.0)) log-weights))
             (z (fold + 0.0 weights))
             (π (make-hash-table equal?))
             (πT (make-hash-table equal?)))
        (define (flow! xs mass)
          (hash-table-update!/default πT xs (λ (m) (+ m mass)) 0.0))
        (for-each
          (λ (xs w)
             (hash-table-update!/default π xs (λ (p) (+ p (/ w z))) 0.0))
          states
          weights)
        (hash-table-walk
          π
          (λ (xs p)
             (when (positive? p)
                   (load-state! ch xs)
                   (let* ((active
                            (filter-map
                              (λ (n i)
                                 (and (not (node-inactive? n)) (cons n i)))
                              nodes
                              (iota (length nodes))))
                          (pick (if (null? active) 0.0 (/ p (length active)))))
                     (when (null? active) (flow! xs p))
                     (for-each
                       (Λ ((,n unquote i))
                          (for-each
                            (Λ ((,x unquote q))
                               (when (positive? q)
                                     (let* ((ratio (forced-step-log-alpha ch n x))
                                            (accept
                                              (if (nan? ratio) 0.0 (min 1.0 (exp ratio))))
                                            (mass (* pick q)))
                                       (flow! (list-replace xs i x) (* mass accept))
                                       (flow! xs (* mass (- 1.0 accept))))))
                            (proposal-support n)))
                       active)))))
        (fold (λ (xs m)
                 (max m
                      (abs (- (hash-table-ref/default πT xs 0.0)
                              (hash-table-ref/default π xs 0.0)))))
              0.0
              (append (hash-table-keys π) (hash-table-keys πT))))))
  (define (proposal-support n)
    (let1 (dk (node-distk n))
          (unless
            (rdistr-independence? (distk-rd dk))
            (hakaru10-error
              'support
              'kernel-residual
              "a random choice whose proposal is not the independence one"
              dk))
          (choice-support dk 'kernel-residual)))
  (define (list-replace xs i x)
    (if (zero? i)
      (cons x (cdr xs))
      (cons (car xs) (list-replace (cdr xs) (sub1 i) x)))))

test/grass: pass

E1. The grass model: rain and a sprinkler may wet the grass, and the grass is observed wet. The exact posterior of rain is upstream's 'theoretical' value.

(define (nnot p x) (if x p 1.0))

(define ((nor strength-x strength-y noise) x y)
  (bern (- 1.0
           (* (nnot (- 1.0 strength-x) x)
              (nnot (- 1.0 strength-y) y)
              (- 1.0 noise)))))

(define grass
  (do/monad
    (← rain (dist bern 0.3))
    (← sprinkler (dist bern 0.5))
    (dist (condition #t (nor 0.9 0.8 0.1)) rain sprinkler)
    (return rain)))

grass = do
  rain        <- dist bern 0.3
  sprinkler   <- dist bern 0.5
  --  dist (nor 0.9 0.8 0.1) rain sprinkler
  grass_is_wet <- dist (True `condition` nor 0.9 0.8 0.1) rain sprinkler
  return rain

-- noisy-or function
nor :: Double -> Double -> Double -> Bool -> Bool -> Distribution Bool
nor strengthX strengthY noise x y =
  bern $ 1 - nnot (1-strengthX) x * nnot (1-strengthY) y * (1-noise)

-- noisy not function
nnot :: Num a => a -> Bool -> a
nnot p True  = p
nnot p False = 1

cf. Examples1.hs:299-314; the expected value is the comment at Examples1.hs:321-322:

-- 0.46815 for 20000 -- with old resampling
-- 0.4684714427203697  -- theoretical
(define (test/grass _)
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     '((#f . 0.53152855727963) (#t . 0.46847144272037))
     (exact-posterior grass)))

((eta 0.0) (memory #(8396592 3852176 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))

test/girl: pass

E2. The eldest daughter puzzle: a family has two kids, one of them a girl (observed through a fuzzy or that errs with probability .01); what is the chance the older is a girl? Exactly 0.495/0.745 = 0.6644295302013423.

(define girl
  (let ()
    (define ((fuzzy2 pfuzz op) x y)
      (let1 (v (op x y))
            (categorical
              `((,v unquote (- 1.0 pfuzz)) (,(not v) unquote pfuzz)))))
    (do/monad
      (← girl1 (dist bern 0.5))
      (← girl2 (dist bern 0.5))
      (dist (condition #t (fuzzy2 0.01 (λ (x y) (or x y)))) girl1 girl2)
      (return girl1))))

girl = do
  girl1 <- dist bern 0.5
  girl2 <- dist bern 0.5
  dist (True `condition` fuzzy2 0.01 (||)) girl1 girl2
  return girl1
 where
   -- Creating a custom distribution
   fuzzy2 pfuzz op x y = let v = op x y in
     categorical [(v,1-pfuzz),(not v,pfuzz)]

E3. Yufei Cai's example of correlated booleans: x and y agree with probability .9 a posteriori, and y alone is a fair coin. The model returns the pair, where upstream returns y.

(define (pcorr/pair pfuzz)
  (define (xor y x) (not (eq? x y)))
  (define ((fuzzy2 pfuzz op) x y)
    (let1 (v (op x y))
          (categorical
            `((,v unquote pfuzz) (,(not v) unquote (- 1.0 pfuzz))))))
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist bern 0.5))
    (← y (dist bern 0.5))
    (dist (condition #t (fuzzy2 pfuzz xor)) x y)
    (return (pair x y))))

pcorr pfuzz = do
  x <- dist bern 0.5
  y <- dist bern 0.5
  dist (True `condition` fuzzy2 pfuzz xor) x y
  return y
 where
   -- Creating a custom distribution
   fuzzy2 pfuzz op x y = let v = op x y in
     categorical [(v,pfuzz),(not v,1-pfuzz)]
   xor y x = not (x == y)

test/ph2: pass

E4. A dependent coin: 0.2 · 0.1 + 0.8 · 0.7 = 0.58, and its parent, with no observation, keeps its prior .2.

(define ph2
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist bern 0.2))
    (dist bern (lift (λ (xv) (if xv 0.1 0.7)) x))))

(define ph21
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist bern 0.2))
    (dist bern (lift (λ (xv) (if xv 0.1 0.7)) x))
    (return x)))

ph2 = do
  x <- dist bern 0.2
  dist bern ((\xv -> if xv then 0.1 else 0.7) <$> x)
-- 0.2 * 0.1 + 0.8*0.7 = 0.58

ph21 = do
  x <- dist bern 0.2
  y <- dist bern ((\xv -> if xv then 0.1 else 0.7) <$> x)
  return x

test/pcond: pass

E5. Mixtures by if_: in pcond2 a branch returns the test's own node, and in pcond4 the else branch observes a fair coin True, halving its weight.

(define (one? x) (lift (cut = <> 1) x))

(define pcond1
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist categorical '((1 . 0.5) (2 . 0.5))))
    (if_ (one? x)
         (dist categorical '((10 . 0.5) (11 . 0.5)))
         (dist categorical '((20 . 0.5) (21 . 0.5))))))

(define pcond2
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist categorical '((1 . 0.5) (2 . 0.5))))
    (if_ (one? x) (return x) (dist categorical '((20 . 0.5) (21 . 0.5))))))

(define pcond4
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist categorical '((1 . 0.5) (2 . 0.5))))
    (if_ (one? x)
         (return x)
         (>> (dist (condition #t bern) 0.5)
             (dist categorical '((20 . 0.5) (21 . 0.5)))))))

pcond1 = do
  x <- dist categorical (pure [(1,0.5), (2,0.5)])
  if_ ((==1) <$> x)
     (dist categorical (pure [(10,0.5), (11,0.5)]))
     (dist categorical (pure [(20,0.5), (21,0.5)]))

pcond2 = do
  x <- dist categorical (pure [(1,0.5), (2,0.5)])
  if_ ((==1) <$> x)
     (return x)
     (dist categorical (pure [(20,0.5), (21,0.5)]))

pcond4 = do
  x <- dist categorical (pure [(1,0.5), (2,0.5)])
  if_ ((==1) <$> x)
     (return x)
     (do
       dist (True `condition` bern) 0.5
       dist categorical (pure [(20,0.5), (21,0.5)]))

test/post-bias: pass

E6. A coin selects which of two normals, centered at 0 and 100, the observation 1 comes from. The likelihood of the observation under N(100, 1) is e⁻⁴⁹⁰⁰·⁵/√(2π), which underflows to 0, so the posterior of the coin is #t, with probability 1.: an observation of weight 0 prunes its path from the search tree, so #f is not in the histogram at all. A model whose every path has weight 0, here 5 observed from the uniform on [0, 1], has the empty posterior '().

(define (post-bias c)
  (do/monad
    (← coin (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ coin
         (dist (condition c normal) 0.0 1.0)
         (dist (condition c normal) 100.0 1.0))
    (return coin)))

An observation whose LL is not finite weighs nothing either, as likelihood weighting drops such a run: in nan-path the normal of standard deviation 0 gives 0 the LL NaN, so k = 0 is pruned and the posterior is the 2 : 1 of the densities at k = 1 and 2, which likelihood weighting visits alone. MH rejects a step to k = 0, whose ratio is NaN and fails both acceptance tests (Metropolis.hs:939-944), but keeps an initial trace of LL NaN, since only an infinite LL makes it rebuild (Metropolis.hs:853-854, 882-891), and then never leaves it: at seed 17 the build draws k = 2, so the chain visits 1 and 2 alone (a regression value), while at seed 110866 it draws k = 0 and the chain stays there, where the oracles and the chain disagree, as upstream's would. In infinite-path the observation's LL is +inf when b is true and -inf when it is false, so no path has a positive finite weight and enumeration, likelihood weighting and the chain all answer '().

(define nan-path
  (do/monad
    (← k (dist uniformly '(0 1 2)))
    (dist (condition 0.0 normal) 0.0 (lift exact->inexact k))
    (return k)))

(define infinite-path
  (do/monad
    (← b (dist bern 0.5))
    (dist (condition 0.0 beta) (lift (λ (bv) (if bv 0.5 2.0)) b) 1.0)
    (return b)))

post_bias c = do
  coin <- dist bern 0.5
  if_ coin (dist (c `condition` normal) 0 1)
           (dist (c `condition` normal) 100 1)
  return coin

_ = mcmC 10 (post_bias 1)
-- All true

cf. Examples1.hs:256-263; P l.427-437.

(define (test/post-bias _)
  (⊦ equal? '((#t . 1.0)) (exact-posterior (post-bias 1.0)))
  (⊦ equal? '() (exact-posterior (dist (condition 5.0 uniform) 0.0 1.0)))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     `((1 unquote (/ 2.0 3.0)) (2 unquote (/ 1.0 3.0)))
     (exact-posterior nan-path))
  (⊦ equal?
     '((1 2) (1 2) ((0 . 30)))
     (list (map car (likelihood-weighting 1000 nan-path))
           (map car (hist (mcmC 1000 nan-path)))
           (hist (mcmC 30 nan-path #:seed 110866))))
  (⊦ equal?
     '(() () ())
     (list (exact-posterior infinite-path)
           (likelihood-weighting 100 infinite-path)
           (mcmC 5 infinite-path))))

test/colored-balls: pass

E7. The colored balls of BLOG (Milch et al., BLOG: Probabilistic Models with Unknown Objects, example 1.1), quoted at ColoredBalls.hs:6-11:

❝
An urn contains an unknown number of balls--say, a number chosen from a Poisson or a uniform distributions. Balls are equally likely to be blue or green. We draw some balls from the urn, observing the color of each and replacing it. We cannot tell two identically colored balls apart; furthermore, observed colors are wrong with probability 0.2. How many balls are in the urn? Was the same ball drawn twice?

Brian Milch et al.

With at most 3 balls and 4 draws, all seen blue, the exact posterior of the number of balls agrees with its closed form: given m balls of which k are blue, a draw is seen blue with probability pₖ = (0.8k + 0.2(m − k))/m, so P(m | n blues) ∝ Σₖ C(m, k) 2⁻ᵐ pₖⁿ. The 8-ball, 10-draw model of upstream has a trace of 2114 nodes and is out of reach of enumeration; its closed form is

((1 . 0.41196368095257)
 (2 . 0.209728624754566)
 (3 . 0.120691976674053)
 (4 . 0.0801847685132436)
 (5 . 0.0590318166419537)
 (6 . 0.046603790739908)
 (7 . 0.0386298621005105)
 (8 . 0.0331654796231948))

next to Hansei's importance sampling, t1 and the average t2 (ColoredBalls.hs:110-147):

let
    [(0.031942788306915014, V 8); (0.0422088248548995876, V 7);
     (0.0481489259210673634, V 6); (0.0611501366775565255, V 5);
     (0.0843776503618006435, V 4); (0.123159075103266638, V 3);
     (0.203898291338526577, V 2); (0.405114307435967658, V 1)] =
 snd (Inference.normalize t1);;

let
  [(0.0318386565368576138, V 8); (0.0384083436381660176, V 7);
   (0.0445344709122082669, V 6); (0.055953476873295431, V 5);
   (0.0777907061915841, V 4); (0.123751456856916756, V 3);
   (0.211354336837841883, V 2); (0.416368552153129956, V 1)]
 = t2;;

The model, with the number of balls a parameter and the balls' colors pre-allocated by replicate/monad, as upstream's sequence . replicate does:

(define (opposite-color color) (if (eq? color 'blue) 'green 'blue))

(define (observed-color color)
  (categorical `((,color . 0.8) (,(opposite-color color) . 0.2))))

(define (balls-prior max-balls)
  (do/monad
    (← balls (replicate/monad max-balls (dist uniformly '(blue green))))
    (return (map cons (iota max-balls 1) balls))))

(define (balls-observe max-balls balls obs nballs)
  (>> (for-each/monad
        (λ (color)
           (do/monad
             (← b (dist uniformly (iota nballs 1)))
             (fold-right
               (λ (i rest)
                  (if_ (lift (cut = <> i) b)
                       (dist (condition color observed-color)
                             (alist-ref i balls))
                       rest))
               (return color)
               (iota max-balls 1))))
        obs)
      (return (void))))

(define (cballs-model max-balls obs)
  (do/monad
    (← balls (balls-prior max-balls))
    (← nballs (dist uniformly (iota max-balls 1)))
    (fold-right
      (λ (i rest)
         (if_ (lift (cut = <> i) nballs)
              (balls-observe max-balls balls obs i)
              rest))
      (return (void))
      (iota max-balls 1))
    (return nballs)))

(define (cballs-closed-form max-balls n)
  (define (binomial m k)
    (if (zero? k) 1 (/ (* m (binomial (sub1 m) (sub1 k))) k)))
  (define (likelihood m)
    (fold (λ (k acc)
             (+ acc
                (* (binomial m k)
                   (expt 2.0 (- m))
                   (expt (/ (+ (* 0.8 k) (* 0.2 (- m k))) m) n))))
          0.0
          (iota (add1 m))))
  (let* ((ls (map likelihood (iota max-balls 1))) (z (fold + 0.0 ls)))
    (map (cut / <> z) ls)))

-- Distribution for the observing of a ball, with the failure rate 20%
observed_color color =
  categorical [(color,0.8),(opposite_color color,0.2)]

maxBalls :: Int
maxBalls = 8

-- Create the prior for the max amount of balls that may be in the urn
balls_prior n = do
  balls <- sequence . replicate n $ dist uniformly (pure [Blue,Green])
  return $ M.fromList $ zip [1..] balls

-- Submodel: Given balls color and the observations, perform conditioning
balls_observe :: M.IntMap (SExp Color) -> [Color] -> Int -> Model ()
balls_observe balls obs nballs = mapM_ observe_ball obs >> return (pure ())
 where
  -- The same ball can be drawn twice as we return the drawn balls
  -- into the urn. A ball keeps its true color, no matter how many
  -- times it is drawn.
   observe_ball color = do
        b <- dist uniformly (pure [1..nballs])
        -- It is more efficient to create if statements, rather than
        -- create an SExp(IntMap Color).
        let observe i =
              let true_color = balls ! i in
              if_ ((== i) <$> b) $
                dist (color `condition` observed_color) true_color
        foldr observe (return (pure color)) [1..maxBalls]

-- The model takes a list of observations as an argument
cballs_model :: [Color] -> Model Int
cballs_model obs = do
  balls  <- balls_prior maxBalls
  nballs <- dist uniformly (pure [1..maxBalls])
  let obs_number i =
        if_ ((== i) <$> nballs) $ balls_observe balls obs i
  foldr obs_number (return (pure())) [1..maxBalls]
  return nballs

cf. ColoredBalls.hs:34-44, 74-99, without the first model that lines 46-72 comment out; W #colored-balls (l.110-119).

  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     '(0.41196368095257
       0.209728624754566
       0.120691976674053
       0.0801847685132436
       0.0590318166419537
       0.046603790739908
       0.0386298621005105
       0.0331654796231948)
     (cballs-closed-form 8 10)))

test/pswitch: pass

E8. pswitch is the counterexample to upstream's reactivation of branches (plan §6.8). The branch-local y depends on m, which keeps changing while y is inactive. The answer is P(c) = 1/2, but the stationary distribution of upstream's kernel gives P(c) = 0.377286. Upstream's argument is at Metropolis.hs:1145-1168.

(define pswitch
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← m (dist uniformly '(0 1)))
    (if_ c
         (dist categorical (lift (λ (mv) `((,mv . 0.9) (,(- 1 mv) . 0.1))) m))
         (diracN (lift identity m)))
    (return c)))

-- (new, plan §6.8; no upstream source)
c ~ bern 0.5
m ~ uniformly [0,1]
if_ c (y ~ categorical [(m,.9),(1-m,.1)])
      (diracN (id <$> m))
return c
(define (test/pswitch _)

test/hansei: pass

E9. The same posteriors from programs written in Hansei itself ((aux hansei), which runs on aux's own delimited control, resetcc). Hansei's grass is its canonical formulation, with the noisy-or spelled out as coin flips (test/hansei.scm:87-100). The soft observation of girl is a coin of the observation's likelihood, and pcond4 is a transcription.

(define (hansei-grass)
  (let* ((rain (probcc-coin 0.3))
         (sprinkler (probcc-coin 0.5))
         (grass-is-wet
           (or (and (probcc-coin 0.9) rain)
               (and (probcc-coin 0.8) sprinkler)
               (probcc-coin 0.1))))
    (probcc-when grass-is-wet rain)))

(define (hansei-girl)
  (let* ((girl1 (probcc-coin 0.5)) (girl2 (probcc-coin 0.5)))
    (probcc-when (probcc-coin (if (or girl1 girl2) 0.99 0.01)) girl1)))

(define (hansei-pcond4)
  (let1 (x (probcc-distribution '((1 0.5) (2 0.5))))
        (if (= x 1)
          x
          (probcc-when
            (probcc-coin 0.5)
            (probcc-distribution '((20 0.5) (21 0.5)))))))

Hansei is described in Embedded probabilistic domain-specific language HANSEI and in Kiselyov and Shan, Embedded Probabilistic Programming, DSL 2009, LNCS 5658; W:111 cites Hansei's implementation of the colored balls.

(define (test/hansei _)

     (exact-posterior grass)

     (exact-posterior girl)
     (hansei->histogram (probcc-reify/exact/a hansei-girl)))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     (exact-posterior pcond4)

test/kernel-residual: pass

E10. The 'extended semantics leaves its target invariant: the kernel residual maxₓ |(πT)(x) − π(x)| is below 10⁻¹² on the finite models below. The colored balls are cut to at most 3 balls and 2 draws, whose extended space has 864 states.

nested-dispatch reaches the deactivation of an observation that the same step activated (deviation 8): subtracting its stale LL there would bias the kernel, with a residual of about .078.

(define nested-dispatch
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist uniformly '(1 2 3)))
    (if_ (lift (cut > <> 1) x)
         (if_ (lift (cut > <> 2) x)
              (dist (condition #t bern) 0.1)
              (dist (condition #t bern) 0.3))
         (dist (condition #t bern) 0.6))
    (return x)))

hakaru10.scm runs the kernel residual on five more models, next to their other checks: act-update and act-update/finite (case B4), whose step activates an observation and updates its parameters at once, and guarded-car, guarded-car/reversed and nested-guarded-car (case B6), whose inactive branches hold a deferred Dirac value.

A state with no active resampleable node stays put (deviation 6): the residual is 0 on a model whose only choice sits in a branch never taken. A support with duplicate points gives a value the weight of all its points, as the sampler does: the residual of uniformly '(1 1 2), whose 1 weighs 2/3, is below 10⁻¹².

As a positive control, the residual exceeds 10⁻³ (it is about .063) on a model whose liar declares a support that disagrees with its density, so that its proposal is not the one the acceptance ratio assumes.

(define (liar)
  (make-distribution
    'liar
    #:log-density
    (λ (x) (log (if x 0.9 0.1)))
    #:sample
    (λ (src)
       (let1 (x (< (rng-double! src 0.0 1.0) 0.9))
             (values x (log (if x 0.9 0.1)))))
    #:support
    (τ '((#t . 0.5) (#f . 0.5)))))

(define liar-or
  (do/monad
    (← a (dist liar))
    (← b (dist bern 0.3))
    (dist (condition #t bern) (lift (λ (av bv) (if (or av bv) 0.8 0.2)) a b))
    (return a)))

A state whose extended LL is NaN or +inf weighs nothing, as exact enumeration prunes it (E6) and no step enters it: the residual is that of the other states. It is still positive on liar-or/nan, the liar next to nan-path's choice, and below 10⁻¹² on nan-path and on plus-inf-state, whose b = 1 has LL +inf and whose posterior is 2/3 and 1/3 on b = 2 and 3.

(define liar-or/nan
  (do/monad
    (← a (dist liar))
    (← b (dist bern 0.3))
    (dist (condition #t bern) (lift (λ (av bv) (if (or av bv) 0.8 0.2)) a b))
    (← k (dist uniformly '(0 1 2)))
    (dist (condition 0.0 normal) 0.0 (lift exact->inexact k))
    (return a)))

(define plus-inf-state
  (do/monad
    (← b (dist uniformly '(1 2 3)))
    (dist (condition 0.0 uniform)
          0.0
          (lift (λ (bv) (case bv ((1) 0.0) ((2) 1.0) (else 2.0))) b))
    (return b)))

With #:semantics 'upstream the kernel is upstream's, whose step delays the updates of inactive nodes (Metropolis.hs:298-303), run from the states where no update is delayed. On pswitch its residual is .05, while the extended kernel's is below 10⁻¹²: the reactivation bias of E8, seen in one step.

T is the independence proposal's (Distribution.hs:29-32), the default kernel: a choice whose distribution has another kernel, such as bern/flip's flip (Distribution.hs:64-70), is a support error, since T would not describe the engine's step. A model where no state has a positive weight, here one that observes #t from bern 0, has no target π: a construction error.

cf. plan §6.11; Design 3 §5.9.

(define (test/kernel-residual _)
  (define models
    `((grass unquote grass)
      (girl unquote girl)
      (pcorr unquote (pcorr/pair 0.1))
      (pcond1 unquote pcond1)
      (pcond2 unquote pcond2)
      (pcond4 unquote pcond4)
      (post-bias unquote (post-bias 1.0))
      (colored-balls-3x2 unquote (cballs-model 3 '(blue blue)))
      (pswitch unquote pswitch)
      (nested-dispatch unquote nested-dispatch)))
  (⊦ equal?
     '()
     (filter-map
       (Λ ((,name unquote model))
          (let1 (residual (kernel-residual model))
                (and (not (< residual 1e-12)) (cons name residual))))
       models))
  (⊦ = 0.0 (kernel-residual (if_ #f (dist bern 0.5) (return 0))))
  (⊦ > 1e-12 (kernel-residual (dist (uniformly '(1 1 2)))))
  (⊦ < 0.001 (kernel-residual liar-or))
  (⊦ < 0.001 (kernel-residual liar-or/nan))
  (⊦ > 1e-12 (kernel-residual nan-path))
  (⊦ > 1e-12 (kernel-residual plus-inf-state))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     `((2 unquote (/ 2.0 3.0)) (3 unquote (/ 1.0 3.0)))
     (exact-posterior plus-inf-state))
  (⊦ < 0.01 (kernel-residual pswitch #:semantics 'upstream))
  (⊦ > 1e-12 (kernel-residual pswitch #:semantics 'extended))
  (⊦= 'support (hakaru10-kind (τ (kernel-residual (dist bern/flip 0.5)))))
  (⊦= 'construction
      (hakaru10-kind
        (τ (kernel-residual
             (do/monad
               (← b (dist bern 0.5))
               (dist (condition #t bern) 0.0)
               (return b)))))))

(aux hakaru10 exact): one model, three interpretations

One model, three interpretations

A Hakaru10 model is a value of the State monad of (aux category state), whose state is the chain: Model a = MCMCM (SExp a) (Syntax.hs:82) and MCMCM a = State MCState a (Metropolis.hs:200). dist, diracN and if_ are models, and do/monad, ← and return combine them as Haskell's do does:

(do/monad
  (← x (dist d args))
  (diracN e)
  (if_ test then-model else-model)
  (return x))

Running a model creates its nodes in bind order, and if_ builds both branches, the inactive one first, as upstream's ifnode does (Metropolis.hs:641-686). condition and lift are not models: conditioning makes an observed distribution (Syntax.hs:174-193), and lifting is pure (Syntax.hs:99-116).

The three interpretations are:

  • the build of mcmC: the model runs once on a fresh chain, as upstream's initial_step' runs it (Metropolis.hs:872-880), and incremental Metropolis-Hastings then updates the graph it built;
  • exact enumeration: a walk of the graph of one build into a lazy Hansei search tree (page hakaru10-exact);
  • the forward sampler: walks of the same graph that draw its active choices in address order and weigh its active observations, for likelihood weighting.

The last two share the graph and the node functions with the engine, but none of its incremental machinery, so each is an oracle of the kernel and of the incremental update (plan §6.10, C37). The tests below run the same models under all three.

test/three-interpretations: pass

H1. The same models under the three interpretations: exact enumeration, 20000 runs of likelihood weighting, and an MCMC chain. Likelihood weighting draws independent samples and lands within .016 of the exact posterior over the seeds 17 and 1-40; the tolerance is .02. The MCMC samples are autocorrelated, and the chain tolerance is .03. Over the same seeds, 20000 samples land within .021 on all but two models, which mix slowly. On pcorr the observation favours x = y nine to one, so a single-site proposal from one likely state to the other must pass through an unlikely one. On the colored balls the number of balls and the balls drawn are strongly correlated (ColoredBalls.hs:46-50). At 20000 samples their distances average .019 and .021 (standard deviations .014 and .010) and reach .070 and .045. Both therefore run 80000 samples, which land within .023 and .025 (means .010 and .011, standard deviations .006 and .005). pswitch runs under 'extended only, since upstream's semantics is biased on it (plan §6.8).

(define (test/three-interpretations _)
  (define models
    `((grass ,grass)
      (girl ,girl)
      (pcorr ,(pcorr/pair 0.1) #:chain-samples 80000)
      (ph2 ,ph2)
      (ph21 ,ph21)
      (pcond1 ,pcond1)
      (pcond2 ,pcond2)
      (pcond4 ,pcond4)
      (post-bias ,(post-bias 1.0))
      (colored-balls-3x4
        ,(cballs-model 3 (make-list 4 'blue))
        #:chain-samples
        80000)
      (pswitch ,pswitch #:semantics extended)))
  (define (disagreement
           name
           model
           #!key
           (semantics 'upstream)
           (chain-samples 20000))
    (let ((exact (exact-posterior model))
          (weighted (likelihood-weighting 20000 model))
          (chain (mcmc-posterior chain-samples model #:semantics semantics)))
      (and (not (and ((histograms-within 0.02) exact weighted)
                     ((histograms-within 0.03) exact chain)))
           (list name exact weighted chain))))
  (⊦ equal? '() (filter-map (cut apply disagreement <>) models)))

((eta 3.154) (memory #(16793184 3865240 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))

test/construction-order: pass

H2. construction-check, the first check of #:check, builds the model, rebuilds it and redraws it, each from its own copy of one rng source. The rebuild must give the same graph (the kind, role, parents and scope of every node), the same values and the same LLs: a model that is not the same each time it runs fails it (plan §15, C35). The redraw draws every resampleable node in address order, from its distribution and its stored parents, and must give back every stored value, and the drawn LLs plus those of the active observations must give the trace's LL within 10⁻⁹. In address order an if_ first has the branch it does not take, whose choices are drawn and whose observations are not weighed, as ifnode builds it (Metropolis.hs:650-661). In 'upstream mode the LL is not bit-identical, because deactivation adds and then subtracts the inactive observations' LL; in 'extended mode the build recomputes it from the redraw's terms, in the redraw's order. So the redraw checks the draw order against the graph and the LL accounting. It runs the engine's construction code, so with the replays of hakaru10.scm it stands for an oracle of the node creation order (plan C18) that would not. #:check runs this check once per chain.

(define nested
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← d (dist bern 0.4))
    (← z (dist normal 0.0 1.0))
    (if_ c
         (do/monad
           (← e (dist bern (lift (λ (zv) (if (> zv 0.0) 0.7 0.2)) z)))
           (if_ (lift (λ (ev dv) (and ev dv)) e d)
                (dist (condition 0.5 normal) z 1.0)
                (>> (dist (condition #t bern) 0.3)
                    (diracN (lift (cut * 2.0 <>) z)))))
         (if_ d (dist normal z 2.0) (dist gamma 2.0 1.0)))))

(define (phier n)
  (fold (λ (_ m) (do/monad (← x m) (dist normal x 3.0)))
        (dist normal 0.0 1.0)
        (iota n)))

(define (normals means sd) (traverse/monad (cut dist normal <> sd) means))

(define (dot xs ws)
  (reduce-right sexp+ 0.0 (map (λ (x w) (lift (cut * <> x) w)) xs ws)))

(define (regression k data)
  (do/monad
    (← w-mean (normals (make-list k 0.0) 2.0))
    (← w (normals w-mean 1.0))
    (← noise-sd (<$> (cut lift (cut / 1.0 <>) <>) (dist gamma 0.5 0.5)))
    (traverse/monad
      (Λ ((,xs unquote y)) (dist (condition y normal) (dot xs w) noise-sd))
      data)
    (return (collect w))))

phier n =
  (iterate (\m -> do {x<-m; dist normal x 3}) $ dist normal 0 1) !! n

cf. Bench.hs:14-15; the regression model of P §3 l.461-504 with Birds1.hs:100-111:

normals :: [SExp Double] -> SExp Double -> MCMCM [SExp Double]
normals means std = mapM (\m -> dist normal m std) means


dot :: [Double] -> [SExp Double] -> SExp Double
dot xs ays = foldr1 (+) $ zipWith (\x ay -> (*x) <$> ay) xs ays
(define (test/construction-order _)
  (define models
    `((grass unquote grass)
      (pcond3 unquote pcond3)
      (pcond4 unquote pcond4)
      (pswitch unquote pswitch)
      (nested unquote nested)
      (colored-balls-8x10 unquote (cballs-model 8 (make-list 10 'blue)))
      (phier-10 unquote (phier 10))
      (regression-5x500 unquote (regression 5 (regression-data 5 500)))))
  (⊦ equal?
     (map (λ (entry) (list (car entry) #t #t)) models)
     (map (Λ ((,name unquote model))
             (list name
                   (construction-check model #:semantics 'upstream)
                   (construction-check model #:semantics 'extended)))
          models)))

test/hansei-format: pass

H3. model->search-tree answers a genuine Hansei search tree. Explored to depth 0 it still has suspended C entries, and explored fully and normalized it is the exact posterior:

(((C #<procedure (a3773)>) 0.35)
 ((C #<procedure (a3773)>) 0.35)
 ((C #<procedure (a3773)>) 0.15)
 ((C #<procedure (a3773)>) 0.15))

Its thunks may be forced in any order: each first restores the states of its path's nodes. Explored level by level, the thunks of each level forced in reverse order, the trees of grass and of pcond4, whose observation is a thunk of its own, give the exact posterior too.

(define (test/hansei-format _)
  (let1 (shallow (probcc-explore 0 (model->search-tree grass)))
        (⊨ (any (λ (entry) (eq? 'C (caar entry))) shallow)))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     (exact-posterior grass)
     (hansei->histogram (probcc-explore +inf.0 (model->search-tree grass))))
  (for-each
    (Λ ((,name unquote m))
       (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
          (list name (exact-posterior m))
          (list name
                (hansei->histogram
                  (probcc-explore
                    0
                    (explore/reversed-levels (model->search-tree m)))))))
    `((grass unquote grass) (pcond4 unquote pcond4))))

test/errors: pass

H4. The run-time checks that replace Haskell's types (plan §7) hold under every interpretation. A model answered by a lifted function (the function of a node, run when the node is built or updated) is a construction error (deviation 9). A non-boolean test is a branch error (SExp Bool, Syntax.hs:275).

(define (test/errors _)
  (⊦ equal?
     '((builder . construction)
       (exact . construction)
       (forward . construction))
     (kinds-under-interpretations
       (do/monad
         (← x (dist bern 0.5))
         (return (lift (λ (xv) (dist bern 0.5)) x)))))
  (⊦ equal?
     '((builder . branch) (exact . branch) (forward . branch))
     (kinds-under-interpretations
       (do/monad
         (← x (dist uniformly '(0 1)))
         (if_ x (return 1) (return 2))))))

test/static-graph: pass

H5. Conditioning on a random choice is ill-typed upstream: observations are external to the model (Syntax.hs:38-42, P §2.3 l.412-421). exact-posterior walks the graph of a build, and the build raises conditioning.

{-
biased_coin_ill_typed_too c1 c2 = do
  b <- dist bern 0.5
  dist (b  `condition` bern) 0.4
  return b
-}

cf. Examples1.hs:196-201. Upstream's types accept a distribution constructor that observes its argument, and its update keeps the observation the node was built with (Syntax.hs:251-252, Metropolis.hs:487-488), so its chain silently observes the first value (deviation 9). Here the engine's update raises conditioning when the observation changes, and so do the oracles, which recompute every node: enumeration, likelihood weighting, the reference evaluator of #:check and the kernel residual all agree with the chain.

(define observes-its-argument
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist bern 0.5))
    (dist (λ (xv) (condition xv (bern 0.8))) x)
    (return x)))

The graph is that of a build, which builds both branches of an if_, so a branch the drawn values leave inactive may raise a parameter error that enumeration, which skips the inactive branches, never meets. It is a dynamic condition, not a breach of the discipline: the build runs in 'extended mode, where it rejects the build (plan C22), and is retried until one completes. So the answer does not depend on what seed 17 draws first, for an error that rejects the build; another error of the build is raised, as in hakaru10.scm's invalid-inactive/branch (case B5). In nested-uniformly, when xs is the empty list, the inactive then branch draws from uniformly '(), an error; with the support of xs in either order the posterior is the same:

(define (nested-uniformly choices)
  (do/monad
    (← xs (dist uniformly choices))
    (if_ (lift pair? xs) (dist uniformly xs) (return 'none))))

and the retry still finds a breach that follows: nested-uniformly/ill-typed observes a choice after the branch, and raises conditioning with the support in either order, also when the first build is rejected.

(define (nested-uniformly/ill-typed choices)
  (do/monad
    (← xs (dist uniformly choices))
    (if_ (lift pair? xs) (dist uniformly xs) (return 'none))
    (← y (dist bern 0.5))
    (dist (condition y bern) 0.5)
    (return xs)))

A model that every build rejects has no graph, so the oracles raise construction after 1000 builds (plan §6.10). Below, the else branch is never taken, but in 'extended mode its normal is drawn as an inactive choice, from a mean that needs (car '()):

(define always-rejected
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ #t
         (return c)
         (do/monad
           (← h (diracN (lift car '())))
           (← mu (diracN (lift cdr (pair h 1.0))))
           (dist normal mu 1.0)))))

Enumeration stops at #:max-paths leaves, with a support error: grass has 4 paths, more than 3, and as many as 4. A tree that model->search-tree builds with #:max-paths counts the leaves of all its explorations, so it can be explored once: the second exploration of grass's tree of 4 paths raises.

A walk of the graph forces the value of an active exit or Dirac node, as the engine's step does (deviation 11), so the oracles fail where mcmC does, whichever branch the graph's build took: in guarded-result the step that makes c false forces the deferred (car '()) of the else branch, which the graph's build leaves inactive, and the walks that take that branch raise the same Scheme error; guarded-result/mirror's build takes it, and raises there.

(define guarded-result
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← r (if_ c (return 'ok) (diracN (lift car '()))))
    (return c)))

(define guarded-result/mirror
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← r (if_ (lift not c) (return 'ok) (diracN (lift car '()))))
    (return c)))

A walk also writes each node's deactivation count before it recomputes the node, which reads it: in guarded-head the then branch's Dirac node, which is not the branch's result, applies car to '() whenever that branch is active, and the walks that take it raise, though the graph's build left it inactive; guarded-head/mirror's build takes it, and raises there.

(define guarded-head
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (let xs (lift (λ (cv) (if cv '(1) '())) c))
    (if_ (lift not c)
         (>> (diracN (lift car xs)) (return 'then))
         (return 'else))))

(define guarded-head/mirror
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (let xs (lift (λ (cv) (if cv '() '(1))) c))
    (if_ c (>> (diracN (lift car xs)) (return 'then)) (return 'else))))

A node that a switch activates may also keep the deferred value it got while inactive, when no update of the step recomputes it: the Dirac node of guarded-head/constant reads a constant, the inner exit of guarded-exit/constant an inner result that does not change, the root of guarded-root/constant has no update. The step forces such a value when its propagation ends, so it raises as the build of that branch active does, and as the walks that take the branch do, in both semantics, with or without #:check, and under the kernel residual: the chain's first sample is 'else, and a later step raises car's error. Upstream, which never reads these nodes, samples 'then and 'else about half the time each.

(define guarded-head/constant
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (← xs (diracN '()))
    (if_ (lift not c)
         (>> (diracN (lift car xs)) (return 'then))
         (return 'else))))

(define guarded-exit/constant
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ (lift not c)
         (do/monad
           (← inner (if_ #t (diracN (lift car '())) (return 'x)))
           (return 'then))
         (return 'else))))

(define guarded-root/constant
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.5))
    (if_ (lift not c)
         (>> (diracN (lift car '())) (return 'then))
         (return 'else))))

(define (test/static-graph _)
  (define observes-a-choice
    (do/monad (← x (dist bern 0.5)) (dist (condition x bern) 0.5)))
  (⊦= 'conditioning (hakaru10-kind (τ (exact-posterior observes-a-choice))))
  (⊦ equal?
     (make-list 4 'conditioning)
     (map (λ (run) (hakaru10-kind (τ (run observes-its-argument))))
          (list exact-posterior
                (cut likelihood-weighting 100 <>)
                (cut mcmC 50 <> #:check #t)
                kernel-residual)))
  (for-each
    (λ (choices)
       (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
          (list choices '((1 . 0.25) (2 . 0.25) (none . 0.5)))
          (list choices (exact-posterior (nested-uniformly choices))))
       (⊦ equal?
          (list choices 'conditioning)
          (list choices
                (hakaru10-kind
                  (τ (exact-posterior
                       (nested-uniformly/ill-typed choices)))))))
    '(((1 2) ()) (() (1 2))))
  (⊦= 'construction (hakaru10-kind (τ (exact-posterior always-rejected))))
  (⊦= 'support (hakaru10-kind (τ (exact-posterior grass #:max-paths 3))))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     (exact-posterior grass)
     (exact-posterior grass #:max-paths 4))
  (let1 (tree (model->search-tree grass #:max-paths 4))
        (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
           (exact-posterior grass)
           (hansei->histogram (probcc-explore +inf.0 tree)))
        (⊦= 'support (hakaru10-kind (τ (probcc-explore +inf.0 tree)))))
  (for-each
    (Λ ((,name unquote m))
       (⊦ equal?
          (list name 'exn 'exn 'exn)
          (list name
                (error-kind (τ (exact-posterior m)))
                (error-kind (τ (likelihood-weighting 100 m)))
                (error-kind (τ (mcmC 100 m))))))
    `((guarded-result unquote guarded-result)
      (guarded-result/mirror unquote guarded-result/mirror)
      (guarded-head unquote guarded-head)
      (guarded-head/mirror unquote guarded-head/mirror)
      (guarded-head/constant unquote guarded-head/constant)
      (guarded-exit/constant unquote guarded-exit/constant)
      (guarded-root/constant unquote guarded-root/constant)))
  (for-each
    (Λ ((,name unquote m))
       (⊦ equal?
          (list name 'else 'exn 'exn 'exn)
          (list name
                (car (mcmC 1 m))
                (error-kind (τ (mcmC 100 m #:semantics 'extended)))
                (error-kind (τ (mcmC 100 m #:check #t)))
                (error-kind (τ (kernel-residual m))))))
    `((guarded-head/constant unquote guarded-head/constant)
      (guarded-exit/constant unquote guarded-exit/constant)
      (guarded-root/constant unquote guarded-root/constant))))

test/nested-branches: pass

H6. Nested if_s under the oracles. A branch answers (diracN d) of a node of the enclosing scope, which the build shares and does not copy (Syntax.hs:164-169), or the diracN of a lifted expression. The else branch observes a coin whose parameter depends on an outer node. The MCMC chains, in both semantics, and likelihood weighting each run 20000 samples and agree with exact enumeration within .02:

(define two-levels
  (do/monad
    (← c (dist bern 0.4))
    (← d (dist uniformly '(0 1 2)))
    (if_ c
         (do/monad
           (← e (dist bern 0.7))
           (if_ e (diracN d) (diracN (lift (cut + 10 <>) d))))
         (do/monad
           (← y (dist categorical '((5 . 0.5) (6 . 0.5))))
           (dist (condition #t bern)
                 (lift (λ (yv dv) (if (= yv 5) 0.8 (/ (+ dv 1.0) 4.0))) y d))
           (return y)))))

((0 . 0.118143459915612)
 (1 . 0.118143459915612)
 (2 . 0.118143459915612)
 (5 . 0.30379746835443)
 (6 . 0.189873417721519)
 (10 . 0.0506329113924051)
 (11 . 0.0506329113924051)
 (12 . 0.0506329113924051))

           exact

           (likelihood-weighting 20000 two-levels))))

test/continuous: pass

H7. Likelihood weighting needs no finite support. pcond3 (E5b) gets its exact answer within .02, and the mean of mixng is 0.5 · 10 + 0.5 · 1 = 5.5 within .4. A run of zero likelihood counts for nothing, so a model whose every run has one, an observation of 5 from uniform 0 1, gives the empty histogram, as exact-posterior does (E6).

(define mixng
  (do/monad
    (← x (dist normal 0.0 1.0))
    (if_ (lift (cut > <> 0.0) x)
         (dist normal 10.0 2.0)
         (dist gamma 3.0 (/ 1.0 3.0)))))

mixng = do
  x <- dist normal 0 1
  if_ ((>0) <$> x)
           (dist normal 10 2)
           (dist gamma 3 (1/3))

cf. Examples1.hs:139-144; P §2.2 l.331-366.

(define (test/continuous _)
  (⊦ (histograms-within 0.02)
     '((1 . 0.5) (20 . 0.25) (21 . 0.25))
     (likelihood-weighting 20000 pcond3))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 0.4)
     5.5
     (fold (Λ ((,x unquote p) ,mean) (+ mean (* x p)))
           0.0
           (likelihood-weighting 20000 mixng)))
  (⊦ equal?
     '()
     (likelihood-weighting 100 (dist (condition 5.0 uniform) 0.0 1.0))))

test/pre-allocation: pass

H8. Pre-allocation, upstream's way to stand for stochastic memoization (ColoredBalls.hs:41-44): replicate/monad creates one coin per key, in order, and a table maps the keys to them. The table is built inside a bind, so each run of the model has its own (plan §15, C35). Enumeration gives each coin its own value on each path, so two pre-allocated coins are independent, and so are the colors of E7's balls.

(define (test/pre-allocation _)
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     '(((#f . #f) . 0.25)
       ((#f . #t) . 0.25)
       ((#t . #f) . 0.25)
       ((#t . #t) . 0.25))
     (exact-posterior
       (do/monad
         (← coins (replicate/monad 2 (dist bern 0.5)))
         (let table (map cons '(a b) coins))
         (return (pair (alist-ref 'a table) (alist-ref 'b table))))))
  (⊦ (equal-approx? 1e-12)
     (map cons '(1 2 3) (cballs-closed-form 3 4))
     (exact-posterior (cballs-model 3 (make-list 4 'blue)))))

(aux hakaru10): colored balls, bird migration and linear regression

Examples

Section 3 of the paper Probabilistic Programming Language and its Incremental Evaluation (APLAS 2016, DOI 10.1007/978-3-319-47958-3_19) evaluates Hakaru10 on two realistic models; quoting design.txt l.454-460:

❝
The Hakaru10 tutorial might have given an impression that Hakaru10 tries so hard to preclude problematic behavior, by restricting conditioning and models, that one cannot do much interesting in it. In this section we briefly evaluate the expressiveness of the language on two realistic models.

Oleg Kiselyov

The two models are the Bayesian linear regression of the PPAML small problems collection (problem 4.1) and the population estimation of Milch et al.'s colored balls. To them the Hakaru10 page adds the bird migration problem of the PPAML challenge, which these tests run on synthetic data (the real data run is hakaru10-birds.scm, opt-in). Upstream's ColoredBalls.hs and Birds1.hs are transcribed definition by definition; the regression exists only in the paper, with its helpers in Birds1.hs.

Each model is a computation of the State monad that threads the chain, as upstream's Model a = MCMCM (SExp a) (Syntax.hs:82), written with do/monad as the Haskell is written with do: dist, diracN and if_ are models, and mcmC runs one under the MCMC engine. The oracles walk the trace graph of one build of the same model: exact enumeration (exact-posterior) and the forward sampler (likelihood-weighting, weighted-samples). So the same cballs-model is checked by MCMC, by likelihood weighting and, on a smaller urn, by exact enumeration, against a closed form; and the generative half of the bird and the regression models, run forward from seed 17, simulates the data that their conditioned versions then explain. The construction check rebuilds the model, which must give the same graph, values and log-likelihoods, and redraws its choices in address order, which must give back the values and the log-likelihood of the initial trace.

Nodes are created when a model runs, in bind order: do/monad top to bottom, and replicate/monad, traverse/monad, for-each/monad and fold/monad head first, upstream's sequence . replicate, mapM, mapM_ and foldM_: creation order is the topological order of the trace (design.txt l.791-794). A chain of if_ is built by fold-right over models, which create no node until the outermost one runs, and if_ delays its branches until it builds them, which reproduces upstream's foldr node for node: at 10000 samples the colored balls' histogram is upstream's own record (hakaru10-replay, X14).

test/colored-balls: pass

C1. Population estimation, from Milch et al.'s BLOG: Probabilistic Models with Unknown Objects (Introduction to Statistical Relational Learning, MIT Press, 2007, pp. 373-398), example 1.1, as ColoredBalls.hs:1-13 quotes it:

❝
An urn contains an unknown number of balls--say, a number chosen from a Poisson or a uniform distributions. Balls are equally likely to be blue or green. We draw some balls from the urn, observing the color of each and replacing it. We cannot tell two identically colored balls apart; furthermore, observed colors are wrong with probability 0.2. How many balls are in the urn? Was the same ball drawn twice?

Milch et al.

The Hakaru10 page (index.html:111) calls it an elegant example of a model with an indefinite number of objects whose real identity is unobservable, a simplified population estimation, which in Hansei makes the case for the memoization of stochastic functions; the paper (design.txt l.519-520) notes that it motivated BLOG, being hard to implement in many probabilistic languages. Hakaru10 bounds the number of balls and pre-allocates one color per ball, which the unused branches marginalize (design.txt l.531-544); its footnote 5 (l.566-569) wishes for lazily instantiated branches instead. Upstream's model, ColoredBalls.hs:28-44 and 74-103:

data Color = Blue | Green deriving (Eq, Show)

opposite_color :: Color -> Color
opposite_color Blue  = Green
opposite_color Green = Blue

-- Distribution for the observing of a ball, with the failure rate 20%
observed_color color =
  categorical [(color,0.8),(opposite_color color,0.2)]

maxBalls :: Int
maxBalls = 8

-- Create the prior for the max amount of balls that may be in the urn
balls_prior n = do
  balls <- sequence . replicate n $ dist uniformly (pure [Blue,Green])
  return $ M.fromList $ zip [1..] balls
-- Submodel: Given balls color and the observations, perform conditioning
balls_observe :: M.IntMap (SExp Color) -> [Color] -> Int -> Model ()
balls_observe balls obs nballs = mapM_ observe_ball obs >> return (pure ())
 where
  -- The same ball can be drawn twice as we return the drawn balls
  -- into the urn. A ball keeps its true color, no matter how many
  -- times it is drawn.
   observe_ball color = do
        b <- dist uniformly (pure [1..nballs])
        -- It is more efficient to create if statements, rather than
        -- create an SExp(IntMap Color).
        let observe i =
              let true_color = balls ! i in
              if_ ((== i) <$> b) $
                dist (color `condition` observed_color) true_color
        foldr observe (return (pure color)) [1..maxBalls]

-- The model takes a list of observations as an argument
cballs_model :: [Color] -> Model Int
cballs_model obs = do
  balls  <- balls_prior maxBalls
  nballs <- dist uniformly (pure [1..maxBalls])
  let obs_number i =
        if_ ((== i) <$> nballs) $ balls_observe balls obs i
  foldr obs_number (return (pure())) [1..maxBalls]
  return nballs

cbr = hist $ mcmC 10000 (cballs_model (replicate 10 Blue))

-- fromList [(1,4265),(2,2203),(3,1244),(4,805),(5,486),(6,291),(7,565),(8,141)]
-- Good agreement

In Scheme, with dispatch the two foldrs of nested if_:

(begin
  (define (opposite-color color) (case color ((blue) 'green) ((green) 'blue)))
  (define (observed-color color)
    (categorical `((,color . 0.8) (,(opposite-color color) . 0.2))))
  (define (balls-prior n)
    (do/monad
      (← balls (replicate/monad n (dist uniformly '(blue green))))
      (return (map cons (iota n 1) balls))))
  (define (dispatch x keys branch otherwise)
    (fold-right
      (λ (key rest) (if_ (lift (cut equal? <> key) x) (branch key) rest))
      otherwise
      keys))
  (define (balls-observe balls obs nballs max-balls)
    (>> (for-each/monad
          (λ (color)
             (do/monad
               (← b (dist uniformly (iota nballs 1)))
               (dispatch
                 b
                 (iota max-balls 1)
                 (λ (i)
                    (dist (condition color observed-color)
                          (alist-ref i balls)))
                 (return color))))
          obs)
        (return (void))))
  (define (cballs-model obs #!optional (max-balls 8))
    (do/monad
      (← balls (balls-prior max-balls))
      (← nballs (dist uniformly (iota max-balls 1)))
      (dispatch
        nballs
        (iota max-balls 1)
        (λ (n) (balls-observe balls obs n max-balls))
        (return (void)))
      (return nballs))))

Given that ten balls were drawn and all looked blue, the posterior of the number of balls has a closed form: with n balls of which k are blue, a drawn ball looks blue with probability pₙ,ₖ = (0.8k + 0.2(n − k))/n, so

P(n | obs) ∝ Σₖ₌₀ⁿ C(n, k) 2⁻ⁿ pₙ,ₖ¹⁰, n = 1, …, 8
(define (cballs-closed-form max-balls obs)
  (define (binomial n k)
    (fold (λ (i c) (/ (* c (- n i)) (add1 i))) 1 (iota k)))
  (define (seen color n k)
    (let1 (blue (/ (+ (* 0.8 k) (* 0.2 (- n k))) n))
          (if (eq? color 'blue) blue (- 1.0 blue))))
  (define (likelihood n)
    (fold (λ (k acc)
             (+ acc
                (* (binomial n k)
                   (expt 2.0 (- n))
                   (fold (λ (color p) (* p (seen color n k))) 1.0 obs))))
          0.0
          (iota (add1 n))))
  (let* ((ls (map likelihood (iota max-balls 1))) (z (fold + 0.0 ls)))
    (map (λ (n l) (cons n (/ l z))) (iota max-balls 1) ls)))

which is

 (6 0.046603790739908)
 (7 0.0386298621005105)
 (8 0.0331654796231948))
(Hansei's importance sampling gives .4164, .2114, .1238, .0778, .0560, .0445, .0384, .0318, ColoredBalls.hs:139-147, and BLOG's Fig. 1.7 agrees). The closed form is first checked against exact enumeration of the same cballs-model on an urn of at most 3 balls with 4 blue observations, (exact-posterior (cballs-model (make-list 4 'blue) 3)), within 10⁻¹²:

; the enumerator walks the trace graph of one build, branching over the support of each active random choice, and jumps over the branches of the dispatch that its tests leave inactive. With 8 balls and 10 observations enumeration is out of reach (the paths number 2⁸ · 8¹¹), so the full model is checked by sampling:
