test/colored-balls/direct: pass

C1′. ColoredBalls.hs:46-72 keeps, commented out, a first version of cballs_model in which every drawn ball is uniform among the nballs balls, a support that depends on a random variable:

{-
Problem with the following model: nballs and b below are strongly correlated.
Hence if nballs is drawn as 5 and b is drawn as, say, 3, all the
proposals to resample nbalss as 1 or 2 will be rejected.

-- The model takes an array of observations as an argument
cballs_model :: [Color] -> Model Int
cballs_model obs = do
  balls  <- balls_prior maxBalls
  nballs <- dist uniformly (pure [1..maxBalls])
  -- The same ball can be drawn twice as we return the drawn balls
  -- into the urn. A ball keeps its true color, no matter how many
  -- times it is drawn.
  let observe_ball color = do
        b <- dist uniformly ((\n -> [1..n]) <$> nballs)
        -- It is more efficient to create if statements, rather than
        -- create an SExp(IntMap Color).
        let observe i =
              let true_color = balls ! i in
              if_ ((== i) <$> b) $
                dist (color `condition` categorical)
                     ((\bc -> [(bc,0.8),(opposite_color bc,0.2)]) <$>
                      true_color)
        foldr observe (return (pure color)) [1..maxBalls]
  mapM_ observe_ball obs
  return nballs
-}

In Scheme the support is (lift (λ (n) (iota n 1)) nballs) and the inlined categorical is observed-color:

(define (cballs-model/direct obs #!optional (max-balls 8))
  (do/monad
    (← balls (balls-prior max-balls))
    (← nballs (dist uniformly (iota max-balls 1)))
    (for-each/monad
      (λ (color)
         (do/monad
           (← b (dist uniformly (lift (λ (n) (iota n 1)) nballs)))
           (dispatch
             b
             (iota max-balls 1)
             (λ (i)
                (dist (condition color observed-color) (alist-ref i balls)))
             (return color))))
      obs)
    (return nballs)))

It is the same model: exact enumeration on the urn of at most 3 balls with 4 blue observations gives the closed form within 10⁻¹²,

((1 . 0.457651641624931) (2 . 0.298386199220923) (3 . 0.243962159154146))
and its graph is smaller, 19 resampleable nodes (8 colors, nballs and one ball per observation) against the 89 of C1, whose branches each draw their own balls. But it mixes badly, for the reason CB:47-49 gives: a proposal of a smaller nballs makes some drawn ball's log-density −∞ and is rejected, while a drawn ball may only move within the current nballs. At 20000 samples from seed 17, nballs moves
26
times, against the
302
of C1's chain (fewer than a fifth), and the posterior of the chain,
((2 0.41845)
 (3 0.01495)
 (4 0.1611)
 (5 0.22275)
 (6 0.0668)
 (7 0.08425)
 (8 0.0317))
misses P(1) = .41196 by more than .2. Hence upstream's dispatch on the number of balls first (CB:91-99), whose branches redraw the balls whenever nballs changes.

test/birds/neighbors: pass

C2. The grid of the PPAML challenge problem: bird migration (Tom Dietterich and Shahed Sorower, version 6, August 30, 2014): cells are numbered in Matlab order, column major (BirdMigrationProblem.txt:103-122, 140), so that on the 4x4 grid of the 1-bird data set cell 1 is the bottom-left corner, 4 the top-left, 13 the bottom-right and 16 the top-right; divMod (n-1) 4 answers the column and the row. A bird flies at most dₘₐₓ = 3√2 ≈ 4.2426 units a night (l.58-59), which Birds1.hs:87-93 compares squared:

neighbors :: CellNum -> [CellNum]
neighbors n = filter (\i -> dist n i <= d_max) [1..nCells]
 where
  dist n m = let (ix,iy) = divMod (n-1) gridDim
                 (jx,jy) = divMod (m-1) gridDim
             in (ix-jx)^2+(iy-jy)^2
  d_max = 18
(begin
  (define (cell->xy n #!optional (dim grid-dim))
    (let1 (m (sub1 n)) (cons (quotient m dim) (remainder m dim))))
  (define squared-distance
    (Λ ((,ix unquote iy) (,jx unquote jy))
       (+ (* (- ix jx) (- ix jx)) (* (- iy jy) (- iy jy)))))
  (define (neighbors n #!optional (dim grid-dim))
    (let1 (from (cell->xy n dim))
          (filter
            (λ (m) (<= (squared-distance from (cell->xy m dim)) 18))
            (iota (* dim dim) 1)))))

The 4x4 grid's diagonal is 3√2 itself, so every cell neighbors every cell, itself included. On a 10x10 grid (the 1000-birds data set, l.202-204) cell 1 reaches the 20 cells

(1 2 3 4 5 11 12 13 14 15 21 22 23 24 31 32 33 34 41 42)


(define (test/birds/neighbors _)
  (⊦ equal? '((0 . 0) (0 . 3) (3 . 0) (3 . 3)) (map cell->xy '(1 4 13 16)))
  (⊦ equal?
     '((0 . 0) (0 . 9) (9 . 0) (9 . 9))
     (map (cut cell->xy <> 10) '(1 10 91 100)))
  (⊨ (every (λ (n) (equal? (iota 16 1) (neighbors n))) (iota 16 1)))
  (⊦ equal?
     '(1 2 3 4 5 11 12 13 14 15 21 22 23 24 31 32 33 34 41 42)
     (neighbors 1 10))
  (⊦ = 20 (length (neighbors 1 10))))

test/birds/transitions: pass

C3. The migration model is log-linear (BirdMigrationProblem.txt:32-33, 60-79): the potential of a flight from cell i to cell j is the dot product of the parameters with its four features, and the probability of the flight is its exponential, normalized over the cells within dₘₐₓ:

θₜ,ₜ₊₁(i, j) = exp βᵀxₜ,ₜ₊₁(i, j) Σⱼ′ exp βᵀxₜ,ₜ₊₁(i, j′)

Birds1.hs computes the numerators, phi (B1:82-85) and theta (B1:95-99), and leaves the normalization to categorical (Distribution.hs:87-91), which divides each weight by their sum; birdpos (B1:76-80) finds the bird where exactly one cell counts it:

-- where is the single bird on the day d of the year y
birdpos :: ObsData -> Year -> Day -> Maybe CellNum
birdpos obs y d = case [i | i <- [1..nCells], obs ! (y,d,i) > 0] of
  [i] -> Just i
  _   -> Nothing
phi :: Year -> Day -> CellNum -> CellNum -> Features -> [Double] -> Double
phi y d from to features bs = sum $ zipWith (*) fs bs
  where
    fs = [ realToFrac $ features ! (y, d, from, to, i) | i <- [1..nFeatures]]
-- multinomial (0; a,b,c) --> 0
-- multinomial (1,theta(i,1),,,theta(i,16))  -->
--     categorical [(1,theta(i,1)),(2,(theta(i,2))),... (16,theta(i,16))]
theta :: Year -> Day -> CellNum -> CellNum -> Features -> [Double] -> Double
theta y d from to features bs = exp $ phi y d from to features bs
(begin
  (define (phi fs bs) (fold + 0.0 (map * fs bs)))
  (define (theta fs bs) (exp (phi fs bs)))
  (define ((transitions features year day) n bs)
    (map (λ (j) (cons j (theta (features year day n j) bs))) (neighbors n)))
  (define (birdpos obs year day)
    (match/first
      (filter (λ (i) (positive? (obs year day i))) (iota n-cells 1))
      ((,i) i)
      (else #f))))

The checks: the weights of the flights from cell 6 on the first night, under β = (1, 2, 0.5, 1), come out of categorical as probabilities summing to 1 within 10⁻¹², each its weight over the total; staying has the features (0 0 0 2), so its weight is e²; birdpos finds the bird on days 1 and 2 (a count of 2 in one cell still is one cell) and not on day 3 (two cells) or day 4 (none).

Each count is a Poisson observation whose intensity is the number of birds in the cell, 0 or 1 with one bird (l.134-138, D:358-367): seeing no bird where it is has LL −1, seeing it once log 1 − 1 = −1, twice log(1/2) − 1; where it is not, seeing none has LL −0 and seeing any has −∞, which pins the bird to the cell where it was seen:

  logDensity (Poisson lambda expl) x
    | x == 0      = -lambda
    | lambda == 0 = minusInf
    | x == 1      = log lambda - lambda
    | x == 2      = log (lambda*lambda/2) - lambda

cf. Distribution.hs:361-365.

(define (test/birds/transitions _))

((eta 0.001) (memory #(12582912 3263336 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))

test/birds/synthetic: pass

C4. The bird migration problem, quoting the Hakaru10 page (index.html:122-128):

❝
Bird migration problem was one of the Challenge problems of the DARPA PPAML (Probabilistic Programming for Advanced Machine Learning) Program. The goal was to estimate the model parameters from the set of bird migration observations over the period of thirty years. The migration model is a log-linear model with four parameters that gives the probability of a bird flying from cell i to cell j under given conditions.

Oleg Kiselyov

The problem statement (BirdMigrationProblem.txt:19-79) models the population as a collective graphical model: every night each bird flies from cell i to cell j with probability θ(i, j), and the birds counted in a cell are Poisson of the birds there. Its first data set has one bird on a 4x4 grid for 30 years of 20 days: each year the bird starts in the bottom-left cell and heads for the top-right one, a fully observed input-output HMM (l.103-105); the task is to estimate β₁, …, β₄ (l.179-182), whose prior may be the absolute value of a zero-mean Gaussian of sd 10, the features being coded so that every parameter should be non-negative (l.457-464). Birds1.hs:50-74 is the model of Sasaki Shoichiro's 2018 bachelor thesis, which, the page says, closely reflects the specification:

model :: Features -> ObsData -> Model [Double]
model features obs = do
  b1  <- abs <$> dist normal 0 10 -- see Statement of Problem, p9
  b2  <- abs <$> dist normal 0 10 -- see Statement of Problem, p9
  b3  <- abs <$> dist normal 0 10 -- see Statement of Problem, p9
  b4  <- abs <$> dist normal 0 10 -- see Statement of Problem, p9
  let bs = collect [b1,b2,b3,b4]
  forM_ [1..nYears] $ \year -> do -- each year
    foldM_ (\n day -> do -- one bird exists in cell n on each day(1..nDays-1)
      let trans n bs = [ (j,theta year day n j features bs) | j<-neighbors n]
      -- n' cell where the bird is on day+1
      let day' = day + 1
      {-
      n' <- case birdpos obs year day' of
          Just nreal -> dist (nreal `condition` categorical) $
                        liftA2 trans n bs
          Nothing    -> dist categorical $ liftA2 trans n bs
      -}
      n' <- dist categorical $ liftA2 trans n bs
      forM_ [1..nCells] $ \i ->
        dist (fromIntegral (obs ! (year,day',i)) `condition` poisson)
           ((\nv -> if i==nv then 1 else 0) <$> n')
      return n'
      ) (1 :: SExp Int) [1..nDays-1]
  return bs
(define (birds features obs #!key (years 30) (days 20) (pin #t))
  (do/monad
    (← betas
       (replicate/monad
         n-features
         (<$> (cut lift abs <>) (dist normal 0.0 10.0))))
    (let bs (collect betas))
    (for-each/monad
      (λ (year)
         (fold/monad
           (λ (n day)
              (do/monad
                (let day′ (add1 day))
                (let trans (lift (transitions features year day) n bs))
                (let nreal (and pin (birdpos obs year day′)))
                (← n′
                   (dist (if nreal (condition nreal categorical) categorical)
                         trans))
                (for-each/monad
                  (λ (i)
                     (dist (condition (obs year day′ i) poisson)
                           (lift (λ (nv) (if (= i nv) 1 0)) n′)))
                  (iota n-cells 1))
                (return n′)))
           1
           (iota (sub1 days) 1)))
      (iota years 1))
    (return bs)))

forM_ is for-each/monad, foldM_ is fold/monad, threading the bird's cell, liftA2 is lift, and the four abs <$> dist normal 0 10 are made by replicate/monad. The test uses upstream's commented-out alternative (B1:62-67), #:pin #t: on a day where the bird is seen its cell is observed, elsewhere it is drawn. The posterior of β is the same, but B1:68 alone draws the cell of every day and needs all the observed ones guessed right for a finite initial trace: on the synthetic data below its build at seed 17 has an LL of −∞, which the construction check reports as skipped.

The synthetic data set has 2 years of 6 days. Its features follow the covariates of the problem's FAQ (l.430-443), with a wind drawn each night from seed 17; its flights are simulated by running the generative half of the model, with β = (1, 2, 0.5, 1), under the forward sampler:

(begin
  (define (synthetic-features years days #!key (seed 17))
    (define (lognpdf x mu sigma)
      (/ (exp (- (/ (expt (- (log x) mu) 2) (* 2.0 sigma sigma))))
         (* x sigma (sqrt (* 2.0 pi)))))
    (define (cosine u v)
      (let ((nu (sqrt (squared-distance u '(0 . 0))))
            (nv (sqrt (squared-distance v '(0 . 0)))))
        (if (or (zero? nu) (zero? nv))
          0.0
          (/ (+ (* (car u) (car v)) (* (cdr u) (cdr v))) (* nu nv)))))
    (define (vector-to p q) (cons (- (car q) (car p)) (- (cdr q) (cdr p))))
    (let* ((src (make-stdgen-source seed))
           (winds (list->vector
                    (map-in-order
                      (λ (_)
                         (receive
                           (angle ll)
                           (distribution-sample (uniform 0.0 (* 2.0 pi)) src)
                           (cons (cos angle) (sin angle))))
                      (iota (* years days)))))
           (top-right (cell->xy n-cells)))
      (memoize
        (λ (year day from to)
           (if (= from to)
             '(0.0 0.0 0.0 2.0)
             (let* ((p (cell->xy from)) (v (vector-to p (cell->xy to))))
               (list (* 4.0
                        (lognpdf (sqrt (squared-distance v '(0 . 0))) 1.0 1.0))
                     (cosine v (vector-to p top-right))
                     (cosine
                       v
                       (vector-ref winds (+ (* (sub1 year) days) (sub1 day))))
                     0.0)))))))
  (define (bird-flights features bs #!key (years 30) (days 20))
    (<$> collect
         (traverse/monad
           (λ (year)
              (<$> (λ (path) (collect (reverse path)))
                   (fold/monad
                     (λ (path day)
                        (<$> (cut cons <> path)
                             (dist categorical
                                   (lift (transitions features year day)
                                         (car path)
                                         bs))))
                     '(1)
                     (iota (sub1 days) 1))))
           (iota years 1))))
  (define ((indicator-counts paths missed?) year day cell)
    (if (and (not (missed? day))
             (= cell (list-ref (list-ref paths (sub1 year)) (sub1 day))))
      1
      0)))

((1 6 8 10 12 12) (1 11 8 15 16 13))

Every third day the watchers miss the bird (a Poisson(1) count is 0 with probability 1/e, l.465-470), so that the cells of days 3 and 6 are latent; birdpos recovers the others. The model has 4 + 2 · 5 · (1 + 16) + 1 = 175 nodes: the 4 parameters and 4 latent cells are resampleable, 6 cells and 160 counts observed, and the answer is a Dirac node. The construction check passes: the initial trace's LL is finite, a rebuild gives the same graph, values and LLs, and redrawing the choices in address order from a copy of the source gives back their values and the LL. One mcmC-fold over 3000 samples sums the β tuples and counts the consecutive ones that repeat, the steps whose proposal was rejected:

2605
of the 2999 do, so the chain moves, and the posterior means are
(4.12945104050349 9.82328450023311 1.3251745427761 2.08709026263613)
finite and non-negative, as the prior makes them. Ten flights are little data, so they are not compared with β, and over seeds 1-20 the chain mixes too slowly for most of them to be bounded: β₄ ranges from 1.3 to 14.7. β₃, the weight of flying with the wind, is pinned by the data alone: it stays between 0.92 and 1.54 on every one of those seeds, so the test asks it to be below 3. As a negative control, the same fold runs on the model with no year of data, (birds features obs #:years 0 #:days days), whose β are the four |N(0, 10)| nodes of the prior, of mean 10 √(2/π) ≈ 7.98: its means are
(7.87600232652835 8.15640125395766 8.22387046673009 8.15729590109516)
and its β₃ is above 3, as it is, between 7.5 and 8.7, on every seed from 1 to 20. The real data run, Birds1.hs:202-215, is hakaru10-birds.scm. Upstream's thesis run took 9 minutes for a million samples, against BLOG's 49 minutes for a thousand (index.html:163-167).

(define (test/birds/synthetic _))

((eta 1.466) (memory #(12582912 3609088 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))

test/regression: pass

C5. Bayesian linear regression, problem 4.1 of the PPAML small problems collection, quoting design.txt l.464-470:

❝
Given the set of training points (x_ij, y_i), i = 1..N, j = 1..k and the generative model y_i = Σ_j x_ij * w_j + noise_i find the posterior distribution on w_j. In the conventional linear regression language, we are given N observations of y assumed to be a linear combination of the controlled quantities x with the parameters w; we have to estimate w.

Oleg Kiselyov

The model is typeset on page 10 of the paper (design.txt l.471-486), here in ASCII Haskell; its helpers, which the paper describes in l.496-504, are Birds1.hs:103-114:

type RegrDatum = ([Double],Double) -- Xs and Y

model :: [RegrDatum] -> Model [Double]
model xsy = do
  let mu = replicate dimK 0
  w_mean   <- normals mu 2
  w        <- normals w_mean 1
  noise_sd <- (1/) <$> dist gamma 0.5 0.5
  let make_cond (xs,y) = dist (y `condition` normal) (dot xs w) noise_sd
  mapM make_cond xsy
  return $ collect w
-- normals means std
-- returns several independently distributed normal random variables
-- whose means are given by the list means, with the same std
normals :: [SExp Double] -> SExp Double -> MCMCM [SExp Double]
normals means std = mapM (\m -> dist normal m std) means


dot :: [Double] -> [SExp Double] -> SExp Double
dot xs ays = foldr1 (+) $ zipWith (\x ay -> (*x) <$> ay) xs ays

collect :: [SExp a] -> SExp [a]
collect = sequenceA

In Scheme collect is exported by (aux hakaru10), sexp+ and sexp* lift the arithmetic, reduce-right is foldr1, and mapM is traverse/monad, or for-each/monad where its results are dropped:

(begin
  (define (normals means sd) (traverse/monad (λ (m) (dist normal m sd)) means))
  (define (dot xs ays) (reduce-right sexp+ 0.0 (map sexp* ays xs)))
  (define (regression data)
    (do/monad
      (← w-mean (normals (make-list (length (car (car data))) 0.0) 2.0))
      (← w (normals w-mean 1.0))
      (← inverse-noise-sd (dist gamma 0.5 0.5))
      (let noise-sd (lift (cut / 1.0 <>) inverse-noise-sd))
      (for-each/monad
        (Λ ((,xs unquote y)) (dist (condition y normal) (dot xs w) noise-sd))
        data)
      (return (collect w)))))

The paper's instance has five hundred points and five parameters, a graph of "511 vertices" (design.txt l.493-495; the hakaru10-language suite counts it). Here twenty points with two features are simulated from w = (1.5, −0.7) and noise sd 0.5 by the forward sampler, from the generative reading of the model with known weights:

(define (regression-data xss w sd)
  (<$> collect
       (traverse/monad
         (λ (xs)
            (<$> (cut lift (cut cons xs <>) <>) (dist normal (dot xs w) sd)))
         xss)))

The graph has 2k + 1 + N = 25 vertices, plus the Dirac node of the answer; the construction check passes. After 20000 samples, the first 2000 dropped as Birds1.hs:205 drops its burn-in, the posterior means of w are

(1.60859970223401 -0.932442673713944)
within .4 of the weights that made the data, and within .04 of the least-squares estimate
(1.62928672635352 -0.926548718746949)
(over seeds 1-12 and 17 they differ from it by at most .021, mostly by less than .01, so .04 is about 4-5 times that spread), which the weak prior barely moves. The noise sd, 1/γ with γ ~ Γ(0.5, 0.5): γ has mean 0.25, so its prior rarely proposes the γ = 2 of the data's sd 0.5 ( P(γ > 2) ≈ 0.005), and the noise sd's posterior sits above 0.5: that widens the posterior of w but does not move its mean.

(define (least-squares/2 data)
  (define (sum f) (fold (Λ ((,xs unquote y) ,acc) (+ acc (f xs y))) 0.0 data))
  (let* ((a (sum (λ (xs y) (* (first xs) (first xs)))))
         (b (sum (λ (xs y) (* (first xs) (second xs)))))
         (d (sum (λ (xs y) (* (second xs) (second xs)))))
         (u (sum (λ (xs y) (* (first xs) y))))
         (v (sum (λ (xs y) (* (second xs) y))))
         (det (- (* a d) (* b b))))
    (list (/ (- (* d u) (* b v)) det) (/ (- (* a v) (* b u)) det))))

(define (test/regression _))

((eta 0.708) (memory #(12582912 3745624 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))

test/plot-mixture: pass

C6. The only use of plot upstream, Examples1.hs:166-167, writes for gnuplot the histogram, in bins of 0.2, of the mixture of Examples1.hs:159-164 (whose KL divergence from mixng the hakaru10-replay suite replays, X12): 5000 samples of a normal of mean 10 and sd 2 followed by 5000 of a gamma of shape 3 and scale 1/3:

_ =
 let n = 10000 in plot (tabulate 0.2 $ mcmC (n `div` 2) (dist normal 10 2) ++ mcmC (n `div` 2) (dist gamma 3 (1/3)))

Upstream writes /tmp/plot.dat; here plot's optional path is a temporary file, read back and deleted. It has the header and one line per bin,

86
lines; the masses sum to 1, and the mean of the bins is
5.42921999999999
within .2 of the mixture's mean (10 + 1)/2 = 5.5 less half a bin, since a bin is the floor of its samples (U:37). The hakaru10-util suite checks the file's format on a toy histogram (cf. U:58-65).

(define (test/plot-mixture _))

((eta 0.024) (memory #(12582912 3278520 1048576)) (stdout "") (stderr ""))
